:PROPERTIES:
:ID:       442c807f-c9d9-45a7-88bf-d38cbbca09cf
:END:
#+title: Parse

* Overview
:PROPERTIES:
:ID:       e16dd5c7-0a63-4c52-afe9-744fdf853643
:END:

We use [[https://github.com/mrkkrp/megaparsec][Megaparsec]] to parse an Org document. The parsing requires us to read in
everything in a single Org file.

We use a *list of /cells/* [fn:cell] for parsing, where each cell can be a

  - [[id:16a68a74-d4cc-41a4-ac77-cbca77de2a31][heading]] (aka [[https://orgmode.org/manual/Headlines.html][headlines]]),

  - [[id:272567ab-4077-4a47-ae78-dfe77243ccc0][paragraph]] ([[id:c8616126-215d-49be-8874-c2aca7d93a54][prose]] text),

  - ordered or unordered [[id:58f69993-599d-4389-ba47-3056b1bb2350][list item]] (can be nested),

  - [[id:cae993e5-02bd-4f89-a6d3-20b41adaff8f][block]] (code block, quotation, example, etc),

  - [[id:d0f60631-8b30-4ae1-992d-49c7b0ea38fb][table]],

  - [[id:a090bf9c-0e52-422b-b0f0-51e3b1bf1698][figure]] (image),

  - LaTeX math fragment ([[id:216e94b7-e037-4f3e-856a-2040d0d0a75a][inline]] or [[id:d1064535-5c98-4c80-a901-5cdc1fd5297f][standalone]]),

  - [[id:4127feee-755d-430e-8fef-192e95143790][footnote]], or

  - [[id:a54a7762-a861-42b1-b2c8-2b760281052c][citation]].

This scheme is good for quickly making sense of the overall document structure
in linear fashion, start to finish. It also simplifies the parsing data
structure --- we just parse one cell after the other. Though using a tree data
structure for the headings could be useful for situations where tree-based
manipulations are desirable, we will never need to do such manipulations for
purposes of weaving the Org input into HTML (or any other format). Hence our
preference for arranging the cells as a list instead of a tree.

In summary an Org document is made up of cells, as well as some
metadata (the title, author name, etc). The doc also has an OID, which
allows other Org files to refer to this Org file.

#+name: data:OrgDoc
#+begin_src haskell
type OrgDoc :: Type
data OrgDoc = OrgDoc
  { oid :: OID,
    cells :: [Cell],
    pseudoEdges :: [PseudoEdge],
    footnoteNumbers :: Map Text Int,
    glossary :: Map Text Int,
    lineLabels :: Map Text Int,
    cslPath :: Maybe FilePath,
    bibPaths :: [FilePath],
    citations :: [Citation],
    meta :: Map Text Text
  }
  deriving stock (Eq, Ord, Generic, Show)
  deriving anyclass (FromJSON, ToJSON)
#+end_src

Footnotes get their own separate data structure called ~footnoteNumbers~ (on top
of the footnote cells in ~cells~), because we have to remember the order of when
these footnotes are referenced from other cells (anywhere we could have prose,
such as paragraphs, headings, etc). This ordering is needed to determine which
number to display as a superscript when creating the link to the footnote
definition. This same ordering information is used in [[id:891f2674-2be9-4e63-b0f8-7ca43f1354fb::f:weaveObject][f:weaveObject]] which
rearranges the footnote definitions so that they are woven in the order they are
referenced, not the order in which they are defined (parsed). This is unlike
every other cell where the order of writing them (cell-by-cell) is preserved
during weaving.

For the ~glossary~, see [[id:73acf466-6941-4c3b-9d6d-e0843d9b0480][Description lists, aka glossary terms]]. We save all
glossary terms we find for this document, so that we can print them out later,
much like citations.

The ~pseudoEdges~ are edges that are ultimately used to create a graph
representation of links between cells. In that representation, we want to use
global cell index numbers to represent the universe of all cells in a
[[id:25827c17-2015-434b-b686-db3d72b2ee4d::data:Archive][data:Archive]]. However, inside the parser for a single Org document, we don't
have access to this global cell index number, as that is computed separately in
[[id:25827c17-2015-434b-b686-db3d72b2ee4d::f:mkArchive][f:mkArchive]]. Also, we don't know the target cell's number either, and the best
information we have is just a [[id:f8254d58-7a3d-4595-a265-e0d6255e6ab5::data:LinkOid][data:LinkOid]]. Because of these issues, we call the
edges here "pseudo" edges. To represent these edges, we use a ~newtype~.

#+name: newtype:PseudoEdge
#+begin_src haskell
type PseudoEdge :: Type
newtype PseudoEdge = PseudoEdge (Int, LinkOid)
  deriving stock (Eq, Ord, Generic, Show)
  deriving anyclass (FromJSON, ToJSON)
#+end_src

The [[newtype:PseudoEdge]] represents a link between a local cell index and a target
cell via the [[id:f8254d58-7a3d-4595-a265-e0d6255e6ab5::data:LinkOid][data:LinkOid]].

The ~deriving anyclass (ToJSON)~ is needed to create the default implementation for
JSON conversion automatically for us. It is the equivalent of writing a default
instance like

#+begin_src haskell
instance ToJSON OrgDoc
#+end_src

but it's shorter because we don't have to put it on its own line, and it's even
more closely coupled with the declaration of the ~OrgDoc~ type itself. If we try
to use ~deriving stock (Eq, Generic, Show, ToJSON)~ we get an error like

#+begin_quote
‘ToJSON’ is not a stock derivable class (Eq, Show, etc.)
#+end_quote

which is why we use the ~deriving anyclass~ variant. All child types also require
the same ~deriving anyclass (ToJSON)~ declaration for this to work. At the end of it
though, we can use the ~encode~ and ~encodeFile~ functions from =Data.Aeson=.

#+name: newtype:OID
#+begin_src haskell
type OID :: Type
newtype OID = OID UUID
  deriving stock (Eq, Ord, Generic)
  deriving anyclass (FromJSON, FromJSONKey, ToJSON)
#+end_src

The OID wraps around a UUID. This way we can be agnostic about the concrete
details of a UUID in the rest of the program. There are some helpers in [[id:5908cefc-0e82-41a5-84ac-1c8c826f5e62][Org ID
(OID)]] for this type, which plays a big role in Lilac because it is the building
block of Org links to headings and other (external) Org documents.

The top-level parsing function is =parseOrgDoc=, which takes some raw text input
and parses it into a rich =OrgDoc= type.

#+name: f:parseOrgDoc
#+begin_src haskell
parseOrgDoc :: Text -> Either Text OrgDoc
parseOrgDoc input = case MP.runParser
  (runStateT orgDocParser defaultOrgParserState)
  ""
  input of
  Left err -> Left . T.pack $ MP.errorBundlePretty err
  Right (orgDoc, _orgParserState) -> Right orgDoc
#+end_src

The [[f:orgDocParser]] is the main parser combinator; it is itself composed of other
parser combinators. All of these parsers live inside the =OrgParser= monadic type,
which is defined as a state monad transformer (=StateT=) with =OrgParserState=,
which wraps around the inner =Parsec Void Text= monad, where =Void= is the custom
error component and =Text= is the type of the text being parsed. Using =Void= for
the custom error component means that we'll be defaulting to the default error
handling mechanism that ships with Megaparsec.

#+name: type:OrgParser
#+begin_src haskell
type OrgParser :: Type -> Type
type OrgParser = StateT OrgParserState (<<Concrete type for Megaparsec>>)
#+end_src

The [[Concrete type for Megaparsec]] feeds the concrete ~Text~ type to use as the
stream for Megaparsec. Megaparsec defines a polymorphic API and expects users to
use concrete types like this for speed optimizations.

#+name: Concrete type for Megaparsec
#+begin_src haskell
MP.ParsecT Void Text Identity
#+end_src

The =OrgParserState= is the custom state we want to use while parsing. You can
think of it as a mutable variable we keep modifying as we make progress during
parsing.

The ~headingLevel~ is the last-seen heading level we parsed. See
[[t:0004-headings-cannot-skip-level]] for how we use it.

The ~headingSectionNumber~ is the last-generated heading section number. This is what we
use to assign each heading its section number.

The =styleMask= is discussed in [[id:b616c1e2-6b42-41cf-8e5d-c1832f95b59b][the section on parsing styled strings]].
The =listLevels= and =listMarkers= are discussed in [[id:58f69993-599d-4389-ba47-3056b1bb2350][List items]]. The
=continuationIndent= is used to keep track of how much indentation to expect
inside a single (contiguous) piece of content; see [[f:paragraphParser]] and
[[f:listHeadIndentLevelParser]] for a usage example.

#+name: data:OrgParserState
#+begin_src haskell
type OrgParserState :: Type
data OrgParserState = OrgParserState
  { docOid :: OID,
    headingOidStack :: [Maybe OID],
    headingLevel :: Int,
    headingSectionNumber :: [Int],
    insideHeading :: Bool,
    styleMask :: StyleMask,
    insideCodeBlock :: Bool,
    insideLinkDisplayName :: Bool,
    listLevels :: [Level],
    listMarkers :: [ListMarker],
    continuationIndent :: Int,
    equationNumber :: Int,
    anonEquationNumber :: Int,
    cellNames :: Set Text,
    cellNumber :: Int,
    pseudoEdges :: [PseudoEdge],
    lineLabelNumber :: Int,
    lineLabels :: Map Text Int,
    lineLabelReferences :: Set Text,
    anonBlockNumber :: Int,
    figureNumber :: Int,
    tableNumber :: Int,
    footnoteNumber :: Int,
    footnoteNumbers :: Map Text Int,
    footnoteDefinitions :: Map Text Prose,
    insideFootnoteDefinition :: Maybe Text,
    prevCellIsFootnote :: Bool,
    glossary :: Map Text Int,
    glossaryTermOffset :: Int,
    insideParagraph :: Bool,
    tanglePaths :: Set Text,
    cslPath :: Maybe FilePath,
    bibPaths :: [FilePath],
    insideCitation :: Bool,
    bracketBalance :: Int,
    insideCitePrefix :: Bool,
    citations :: [Citation],
    orgParserConf :: OrgParserConf
  }
  deriving stock (Eq, Show)
#+end_src

#+name: f:defaultOrgParserState
#+begin_src haskell
defaultOrgParserState :: OrgParserState
defaultOrgParserState =
  OrgParserState
    { docOid = nilOid,
      headingOidStack = [],
      headingLevel = 0,
      headingSectionNumber = [],
      insideHeading = False,
      styleMask = styleMaskFrom [],
      insideCodeBlock = False,
      insideLinkDisplayName = False,
      listLevels = [],
      listMarkers = [],
      continuationIndent = 0,
      equationNumber = 0,
      anonEquationNumber = 0,
      cellNames = Set.empty,
      cellNumber = 0,
      pseudoEdges = [],
      lineLabelNumber = 0,
      lineLabels = M.empty,
      lineLabelReferences = Set.empty,
      anonBlockNumber = 0,
      figureNumber = 0,
      tableNumber = 0,
      footnoteNumber = 0,
      footnoteNumbers = M.empty,
      footnoteDefinitions = M.empty,
      insideFootnoteDefinition = Nothing,
      prevCellIsFootnote = False,
      glossary = M.empty,
      glossaryTermOffset = 0,
      insideParagraph = False,
      tanglePaths = Set.empty,
      cslPath = Nothing,
      bibPaths = [],
      insideCitation = False,
      bracketBalance = 0,
      insideCitePrefix = False,
      citations = [],
      orgParserConf = defaultOrgParserConf
    }
#+end_src

The ~anonBlockNumber~ is used to give some unique name (in lieu of actual names)
for anonymous blocks. This is needed for generating link anchors for it during
weaving.

* =Lilac.Parse= module
:PROPERTIES:
:ID:       5ce50c9a-5413-48d9-8bba-e465a4eb527c
:END:

The parsing mechanism is in [[file:../src/Lilac/Parse.hs]], which we describe below.
We discuss each parser and interleave some tests for the smaller parsers. If you
are curious about the overall testing framework itself (where and how these
interleaved tests are executed), take a look at the [[id:ebf0d269-79d3-408a-a6e7-baf720d73837][Testing framework]].

* Whitespace and comments
:PROPERTIES:
:ID:       fbca0c4e-c568-4c12-b89c-7cf334b8d50f
:END:

Before writing our first parsers, we have to consider the problem of whitespace
and comments.

Any syntax that is expected to be used by humans have some designation for
whitespace to separate meaningful tokens from each other. One way to deal with
(necessary) whitespace is to bake in whitespace parsers into each token we want
to parse. This gets cumbersome so we want to instead be able to teach parsers to
be able to skip over trailing whitespace. This way, these parsers will all know
how to deal with whitespace in a uniform way. For example, this is what we do in
[[f:cellParser]] where we wrap all of the major =Cell= parsers with a =lexeme=.

A slightly separate problem is how to deal with comments. In a sense they are
just like whitespace because they hold no meaningful information on their own.
Luckily, =Text.Megaparsec.Char.Lexer= provides a =space= helper which can create a
parser that skips over whitespace as well as comments. This is what we use below
to define =skipNewlinesAndComments=. See [[https://markkarpov.com/tutorial/megaparsec.html#lexing][the Megaparsec tutorial]] for more
information.

#+name: f:skipNewlinesAndComments
#+begin_src haskell
skipNewlinesAndComments :: OrgParser ()
skipNewlinesAndComments =
  L.space
    whiteSpaceParser
    singleLineCommentParser
    blockCommentParser
  where
    whiteSpaceParser :: OrgParser ()
    whiteSpaceParser = do
      _ <- MP.takeWhile1P (Just "newline") (== '\n')
      orgParserState <- get
      _ <-
        MP.optional
          . MP.label "indentation for next thing"
          $ MP.count orgParserState.continuationIndent (char ' ')
      pure ()
    singleLineCommentParser :: OrgParser ()
    singleLineCommentParser = L.skipLineComment "# "
    blockCommentParser :: OrgParser ()
    blockCommentParser = L.skipBlockComment "#+begin_comment\n" "#+end_comment\n"
#+end_src

The three arguments to =L.space= (where =L= stands for =Text.Megaparsec.Char.Lexer=)
are the parsers for whitespace, single-line comments, and block comments.
=L.space= will try to repeatedly apply the three given parsers; it only stops
parsing when faced with input where all three parsers fail.

We only parse newline characters in =whiteSpaceParser= above. We don't parse
spaces. This is because we do not want to skip over any /indentation/ --- that is,
space characters immediately following a newline. Such indentation could be an
error (for example, for headings or paragraphs), or a necessary part of a cell
(such as =CListItem= cells in [[f:listItemsEndParser]]), and so we leave that
determination up to other parsers.

It's important that we use =takeWhile1P= for =whiteSpaceParser= (where the =1= denotes
that it requires at least 1 newline character) because otherwise if it is
allowed to accept 0 newlines, it may loop forever upon seeing non-newline input.

The ="# "= for =singleLineCommentParser= signifies that single-line comments start
with a pound sign and a space. The =blockCommentParser= uses =L.skipBlockComment= to
denote a region of text which is considered a commented area. We include
explicit newlines in =blockCommentParser= because we want to ensure that block
comment markers are always on their own line (e.g., ~#+end_comment~ is valid, but
~#+end_comment foo~ is not).

=Text.Megaparsec.Char.Lexer= also defines a =L.skipBlockCommentNested= helper, which
is able to understand if the ending marker of a block (=#+end_comment\n= for us)
should be ignored because it's nested and instead skip past it to find the true
(non-nested) ending marker. That's a little too complicated (and it's probably
not very commonly needed). So we use the non-nested =L.skipBlockComment= to keep
the syntax simple.

Lastly we have =lexeme=, which wraps around any other parser to make it consume
trailing /newlines and comments/. This is used in [[f:cellParser]]. Note that it
does not consume any trailing spaces, because space characters are important for
detecting indentation and we wouldn't want to skip past them.

#+name: f:lexeme
#+begin_src haskell
lexeme :: OrgParser a -> OrgParser a
lexeme = L.lexeme skipNewlinesAndComments
#+end_src

The =orgDocParser= first needs to parse any metadata associated with the document,
such as the document ID, title and author. It starts by skipping any leading
whitespace/comments. We have to do this first because all of the other parsers
we use will follow the pattern of parsing the thing we want, followed by
skipping over as much trailing whitespace as possible. In other words, none of
the parsers are able to deal with leading whitespace/comments, so we have to
deal with it here separately.

#+name: f:orgDocParser
#+begin_src haskell
orgDocParser :: OrgParser OrgDoc
orgDocParser = do
  _ <- optional skipNewlinesAndComments
  (docOid, cslPath, bibPaths, meta) <- docMetaParser
  _ <- lilacExtensionsParser
  cells <- many cellParser
  MP.eof
  lineLabels <- reconcileLineLabels
  footnoteNumbers <- reconcileFootnotes
  citations <- collectCitations
  orgParserState <- get
  resetState
  let meaningfulCell :: Cell -> Bool
      meaningfulCell = \case
        CListItem [] -> False
        CHeading {}
        CListItem {}
        CParagraph {}
        CGlossaryTerm {}
        CBlock {}
        CMathFragment {}
        CFigure {}
        CTable {}
        CFootnote {}
        CCommand {} -> True
  let meaningfulCells = filter meaningfulCell cells
  when (orgParserState.cellNumber /= length meaningfulCells)
    $ fail ("mismatch: cellNumber: " <> show orgParserState.cellNumber <> " len meaningfulCells: " <> (show $ length meaningfulCells))
  pure
    $ OrgDoc
      { oid = docOid,
        cells = filter meaningfulCell cells,
        pseudoEdges = orgParserState.pseudoEdges,
        footnoteNumbers = footnoteNumbers,
        glossary = orgParserState.glossary,
        lineLabels = lineLabels,
        cslPath = cslPath,
        bibPaths = bibPaths,
        citations = citations,
        meta = deleteRedundantKeys meta ["cite_export"]
      }
#+end_src

The =orgDocParser= delegates most of the work to the [[f:cellParser]], which in turn
delegates work again to the individual parsers that know how to parse different
cells. We have to filter out cells that look like =CListItem []= in ~meaningfulCell~
because that can happen when [[f:listItemsEndParser]] detects maximum indentation
that matches the most-deeply-nested item (in which case no list items end).

See [[f:docMetaParser]] to see how ~docMetaParser~ works.

We call [[f:resetState]] to clear some state from [[data:OrgParserState]], because
otherwise it gets too polluted during testing. Also, it's not like we're
throwing away information because everything we need is still captured elsewhere
(just check the fields that have the same name in both [[data:OrgParserState]] and
[[data:OrgDoc]]).

#+name: f:resetState
#+begin_src haskell
resetState :: OrgParser ()
resetState =
  modify $ \s ->
    s
      { OrgParserState.bibPaths = [],
        OrgParserState.citations = [],
        OrgParserState.cslPath = Nothing,
        OrgParserState.cellNumber = 0,
        OrgParserState.pseudoEdges = []
      }
#+end_src

In [[f:resetState]] we cannot simply refer to the field alone in the record update,
like this

#+begin_src haskell
  modify (\s -> s {citations = []})
#+end_src

because it will result in an [[https://errors.haskell.org/messages/GHC-02256/]["Ambiguous record update" error]], because both
[[data:OrgDoc]] and [[data:OrgParserState]] have a field named ~citations~. So we work
around this by importing the latter through ~import Lilac.Types as OrgParserState~
in [[module:Lilac.Parse]]. See [[https://discourse.haskell.org/t/what-are-you-supposed-to-do-about-wambiguous-fields/9545][this discussion from 2024]] for more information.

In a similar vein to [[f:resetState]], [[f:deleteRedundantKeys]] removes certain entries
from the given map data structure. In the case of [[f:orgDocParser]], it removes
keys that won't be used because the information in those keys have already been
parsed and saved elsewhere.

#+name: f:deleteRedundantKeys
#+begin_src haskell
deleteRedundantKeys :: (Ord k) => Map k v -> [k] -> Map k v
deleteRedundantKeys = foldl' (flip M.delete)
#+end_src

We should be able to parse empty input.

#+name: t:empty-input
#+begin_src haskell
it "empty input" $ do
  MP.runParser
    (runStateT L.orgDocParser defaultOrgParserState)
    "empty input"
    nilOidOrgDoc
    `shouldParse` (L.defaultOrgDoc nilOid [], defaultOrgParserState)
#+end_src

The =defaultOrgDoc= is just a default (mostly blank) ~OrgDoc~. It's useful for
testing.

#+name: f:defaultOrgDoc
#+begin_src haskell
defaultOrgDoc :: OID -> [(Text, Text)] -> OrgDoc
defaultOrgDoc oid metas =
  OrgDoc
    { oid = oid,
      cells = [],
      pseudoEdges = [],
      footnoteNumbers = M.empty,
      glossary = M.empty,
      lineLabels = M.empty,
      cslPath = Nothing,
      bibPaths = [],
      citations = [],
      meta = fromList metas'
    }
  where
    metas' =
      if oid == nilOid
        then ("ID", "00000000-0000-0000-0000-000000000000") : metas
        else metas
#+end_src

We should also be able to parse blank documents (not empty, but only has
whitespace).

#+name: ti:0001-blankline
#+header: :tangle test/Lilac/ParseSpec/0001-blankline
#+begin_src org


#+end_src

We have to put in two newlines above because during parsing we will lose one
newline (it is used to parse the ~"\n#+end_src"~ string; see [[f:someBTokensParser]]).
This leaves us with just one newline in
[[file:../test/Lilac/ParseSpec/0001-blankline]].

#+name: t:0001-blankline
#+begin_src haskell
shouldParseWith
  "0001-blankline"
  (L.defaultOrgDoc nilOid [])
#+end_src

Aside: [[ti:0001-blankline]] in HTML looks like there is no content (this is
intentional), because [[f:zeroBTokensParser]] parses it the same way it parses a
block with zero bytes in it (see [[ti:0007-blocks-empty]]).

Parsing comments should also result in a blank document.

#+name: ti:0001-comments-only
#+header: :tangle test/Lilac/ParseSpec/0001-comments-only
#+begin_src org
# This is a single-line comment.

,#+begin_comment
This is inside
a block comment.
,#+end_comment
#+end_src

#+name: t:0001-comments-only
#+begin_src haskell
shouldParseWith
  "0001-comments-only"
  (L.defaultOrgDoc nilOid [])
#+end_src

At the other end of the spectrum, Lilac should be able to parse itself
(=lilac.org=).

#+name: t:self-parse
#+begin_src haskell
before (LU.readFrom "" "base/lilac.org") $ do
  it "self-parse" $ \input -> do
    MP.runParser
      (runStateT L.orgDocParser defaultOrgParserState)
      "base/lilac.org"
      `shouldSucceedOn` input
#+end_src

* =docMetaParser=

An Org document's metadata is the first meaningful thing that could be parsed.
Metadata can be created with a ~:PROPERTIES:~ drawer, or with a line that looks
like =#+key: value= where =key= is a string and =value= is a series of tokens (it can
have multiple words in it). The key and value syntax is trickier, so we look at
that first with =docMetaEntryParser= below.

#+name: f:docMetaEntryParser
#+begin_src haskell
docMetaEntryParser :: OrgParser (Text, Text)
docMetaEntryParser = do
  avoidParsingBlockMetadata
  avoidParsingLilacExtensions
  _ <- string "#+"
  key <- MP.takeWhile1P (Just "(doc meta) keyword letter") isKeywordLetter
  _ <- MP.label "(doc meta) separator" $ string ": "
  _ <- many $ char ' '
  value <- MP.takeWhile1P (Just "(doc meta) value letter") (\c -> c /= '\n')
  pure (key, value)
  where
    isKeywordLetter c = or $ map ($ c) [isAsciiLower, (== '_')]
    avoidParsingBlockMetadata :: OrgParser ()
    avoidParsingBlockMetadata =
      mapM_
        MP.notFollowedBy
        $ map
          string
          [ "#+begin_",
            "#+name: ",
            "#+header: ",
            "#+print_bibliography:",
            "#+print_glossary:"
          ]
    avoidParsingLilacExtensions :: OrgParser ()
    avoidParsingLilacExtensions = MP.notFollowedBy $ string "#+lilac_"
#+end_src

Note how it also has to tiptoe around blocks by checking that it is not looking
at block-related syntax with =avoidParsingBlockMetadata=. This is important
because otherwise if an Org document begins with a [[id:cae993e5-02bd-4f89-a6d3-20b41adaff8f][code block]] as the first
thing (before [[id:16a68a74-d4cc-41a4-ac77-cbca77de2a31][headings]] or anything else), we may consume that as part of
~docMetaParser~ and fail because we think we are expecting ~: ~ followed by a
value.

Similarly, we also do not parse any [[id:84edd33a-a146-4a46-ac0a-cd1c3d0926d3][Lilac extensions]] because they can only be
parsed by the [[f:lilacExtensionsParser]].

The [[f:docMetaParser]] first checks for the top-level ~:PROPERTIES:~ drawer with
[[f:propertiesDrawerParser]]. Then it just parses =docMetaEntryParser= multiple times,
while consuming any trailing whitespace or comments with [[f:lexeme]].

#+name: f:docMetaParser
#+begin_src haskell
docMetaParser :: OrgParser (OID, Maybe FilePath, [FilePath], Map Text Text)
docMetaParser = do
  topLevelDrawerProps <- propertiesDrawerParser
  _ <- optional skipNewlinesAndComments
  metaKVsList <- many $ lexeme docMetaEntryParser
  let (bibPathsText, remainingKVs) = collectValsFor "bibliography" metaKVsList
      metaKVs = fromList remainingKVs
      bibPaths = map T.unpack bibPathsText
  let conflictingKeys = keys $ M.intersection topLevelDrawerProps metaKVs
  when (not $ null conflictingKeys)
    $ fail
    $ "conflicting keys in the top-level PROPERTIES drawer and document metadata key/values: "
    <> (show conflictingKeys)
  case lookup "ID" topLevelDrawerProps of
    Nothing -> fail $ "required document ID property not found"
    Just oidText -> do
      oid <- validateOidText oidText
      cslPath <- case lookup "cite_export" metaKVs of
        Just maybeCslPath -> validateCslPath maybeCslPath
        Nothing -> pure Nothing
      modify $ \s ->
        s
          { OrgParserState.bibPaths = bibPaths,
            OrgParserState.cslPath = cslPath,
            docOid = oid
          }
      pure (oid, cslPath, bibPaths, topLevelDrawerProps <> metaKVs)
#+end_src

OID validation in [[f:validateOidText]] delegates to the ~Lilac.Types.fromText~
helper.

#+name: f:validateOidText
#+begin_src haskell
validateOidText :: (MonadFail m) => Text -> m OID
validateOidText oidText = case Lilac.Types.fromText oidText of
  Nothing -> fail $ show oidText <> " is not a valid OID"
  Just oid -> pure oid
#+end_src

Note that using the properties drawer is the main way in which the ~ID~ field of a
document is created, which is then used by Orgmode (see
~org-id-link-to-org-use-id~, ~org-id-link-consider-parent-id~, and
~org-id-locations-file~). Using ID's for things (instead of the file name) allows
us to create links that are more resilient against file name changes (or
directory changes). See [[id:ca12051e-cd17-4d8d-996a-767695441d48][Drawers]] for more discussion about properties drawers and
[[ti:0002-doc-metadata-properties-drawer]].

The special key ~#+cite_export: ...~ is parsed separately as the ~cslPath~, using
the [[f:validateCslPath]] helper. This is needed for supporting [[id:a54a7762-a861-42b1-b2c8-2b760281052c][Citations]].

** Lilac extensions
:PROPERTIES:
:ID:       84edd33a-a146-4a46-ac0a-cd1c3d0926d3
:END:

Lilac supports extensions to alter the behavior of the parser for the
document being parsed. These extensions are all prefixed by ~lilac_~ and are set
like other document metadata; they must come *after* the other (typical) Org metadata.

Below is a list of supported extensions:

  - ~lilac_maxHeadingLevel~ :: [[f:maxHeadingLevelParser]] This takes an integer as a
    value. Using ~-1~ means no headings are allowed at all. Using ~0~ means headings
    can have as many asterisks as desired. Using a positive integer means
    headings may only have that many (or less) asterisks; otherwise Lilac will
    fail the parse.

  - ~lilac_maxListLevel~ :: [[f:maxListLevelParser]] This has the same semantics as
    ~lilac_maxHeadingLevel~ but is for list items, not headings.

#+name: data:OrgParserConf
#+begin_src haskell
type OrgParserConf :: Type
data OrgParserConf = OrgParserConf
  { maxHeadingLevel :: Int,
    maxListLevel :: Int
  }
  deriving stock (Eq, Show)
#+end_src

#+name: f:defaultOrgParserConf
#+begin_src haskell
defaultOrgParserConf :: OrgParserConf
defaultOrgParserConf =
  OrgParserConf
    { maxHeadingLevel = 3,
      maxListLevel = 3
    }
#+end_src

Parsing Lilac extensions involves parsing known key/value combinations, and
updating the ~OrgParserState~ accordingly.

#+name: f:lilacExtensionsParser
#+begin_src haskell
lilacExtensionsParser :: OrgParser ()
lilacExtensionsParser = do
  void
    . many
    $ MP.choice
      [ maxHeadingLevelParser,
        maxListLevelParser
      ]
#+end_src

The [[f:maxHeadingLevelParser]] resembles the [[f:docMetaEntryParser]], but the main
difference is that it does not return anything (its type is ~OrgParser ()~).
Instead it just modifies the ~orgParserState~.

#+name: f:maxHeadingLevelParser
#+begin_src haskell
maxHeadingLevelParser :: OrgParser ()
maxHeadingLevelParser = do
  orgParserState <- get
  _ <- string $ "#+lilac_maxHeadingLevel: "
  _ <- many $ char ' '
  val <- signedIntParser
  let opc = orgParserState.orgParserConf {maxHeadingLevel = val}
  put orgParserState {orgParserConf = opc}
#+end_src

The ~signedIntParser~ knows how to parse a signed integer. The
~parserBetweenSignAndNumber~ is the parser for parsing the area between the sign
(~-~) and the number. Here we define it as ~pure ()~ meaning that nothing is
allowed. The usual use case here is to use a whitespace parser, so as to allow a
space between the sign and number, but we don't do this here for simplicity.

#+name: f:signedIntParser
#+begin_src haskell
signedIntParser :: OrgParser Int
signedIntParser = L.signed parserBetweenSignAndNumber number
  where
    number :: OrgParser Int
    number = L.lexeme skipNewlinesAndComments $ L.decimal
    parserBetweenSignAndNumber :: OrgParser ()
    parserBetweenSignAndNumber = pure ()
#+end_src

Although we don't "return" anything (the type signature of
[[f:lilacExtensionsParser]] is just ~OrgParser ()~), we still use ~put~ inside the
parsers so that they modify the [[data:OrgParserState]].

#+name: f:maxListLevelParser
#+begin_src haskell
maxListLevelParser :: OrgParser ()
maxListLevelParser = do
  orgParserState <- get
  _ <- string $ "#+lilac_maxListLevel: "
  _ <- many $ char ' '
  val <- signedIntParser
  let opc = orgParserState.orgParserConf {maxListLevel = val}
  put orgParserState {orgParserConf = opc}
#+end_src

Unsurprisingly, [[f:maxListLevelParser]] closely resembles [[f:maxHeadingLevelParser]].

** Tests

Below are some unit test cases for parsing metadata.

#+name: ti:0002-doc-metadata:basic
#+header: :tangle test/Lilac/ParseSpec/0002-doc-metadata
#+begin_src org
,#+title: An Org Document
,#+author: A. U. Thor
,#+property: header-args+ :noweb no-export
,#+auto_tangle: t
#+end_src

#+name: t:0002-doc-metadata:basic
#+begin_src haskell
shouldParseWith
  "0002-doc-metadata"
  $ L.defaultOrgDoc
    nilOid
    [ ("author", "A. U. Thor"),
      ("auto_tangle", "t"),
      ("property", "header-args+ :noweb no-export"),
      ("title", "An Org Document")
    ]
#+end_src

Note that we currently don't do any intelligent processing of the metadata
fields. For example, the value of the =property= key above is a mess because it
itself contains a key and value (=:noweb= and =no-export=).

Below is the same as the test above, but with some whitespace and comments
sprinkled in between.

#+name: ti:0003-doc-metadata-with-comments
#+header: :tangle test/Lilac/ParseSpec/0003-doc-metadata-with-comments
#+begin_src org
,#+title: An Org Document
# This is a comment line.
,#+author: A. U. Thor
# This is another comment line.


# Note the whitespace above this line.
,#+property: header-args+ :noweb no-export
# Below is a block comment.
,#+begin_comment
Yet another comment.
,#+end_comment
,#+auto_tangle: t

# Last comment followed by some trailing newlines.





#+end_src

#+name: t:0003-doc-metadata-with-comments
#+begin_src haskell
shouldParseWith
  "0003-doc-metadata-with-comments"
  $ L.defaultOrgDoc
    nilOid
    [ ("author", "A. U. Thor"),
      ("auto_tangle", "t"),
      ("property", "header-args+ :noweb no-export"),
      ("title", "An Org Document")
    ]
#+end_src

Indentation is invalid for document metadata. Even single-line comments may not
be indented.

#+name: ti:0008-indentation-comment
#+header: :tangle test/Lilac/ParseSpec/0008-indentation-comment
#+begin_src org
 # invalid comment (it's indented)
#+end_src

#+name: t:0008-indentation-comment
#+begin_src haskell
shouldFailWithMessage
  "0008-indentation-comment"
  "invalid indentation"
  1
#+end_src

Metadata values can have leading whitespace. We ignore such whitespace.

#+name: ti:0002-doc-metadata-whitespace-before-value
#+header: :tangle test/Lilac/ParseSpec/0002-doc-metadata-whitespace-before-value
#+begin_src org
,#+title:             Parse me without leading whitespace
#+end_src

#+name: t:0002-doc-metadata-whitespace-before-value
#+begin_src haskell
shouldParseWith
  "0002-doc-metadata-whitespace-before-value"
  $ L.defaultOrgDoc
    nilOid
    [ ("title", "Parse me without leading whitespace")
    ]
#+end_src

Metadata values are required (there must be some non-whitespace text for it).

#+name: ti:0002-doc-metadata-missing-value
#+header: :tangle test/Lilac/ParseSpec/0002-doc-metadata-missing-value
#+begin_src org
,#+title:
#+end_src

#+name: t:0002-doc-metadata-missing-value
#+begin_src haskell
shouldFailWithError
  "0002-doc-metadata-missing-value"
  (utoks ":\n" <> elabel "(doc meta) separator" <> elabel "(doc meta) keyword letter")
  7
#+end_src

Metadata can also come from top-level properties drawers.

#+name: ti:0002-doc-metadata-properties-drawer
#+header: :tangle test/Lilac/ParseSpec/0002-doc-metadata-properties-drawer
#+begin_src org
:PROPERTIES:
:ID:       7af1aa3e-41ca-4810-a001-2fdf10768f2f
:END:
#+end_src

#+name: t:0002-doc-metadata-properties-drawer
#+begin_src haskell
shouldParseWithState
  "0002-doc-metadata-properties-drawer"
  ( L.defaultOrgDoc
      (fromWords 0x7af1aa3e41ca4810 0xa0012fdf10768f2f)
      [ ("ID", "7af1aa3e-41ca-4810-a001-2fdf10768f2f")
      ]
  )
  defaultOrgParserState
    { docOid = fromWords 0x7af1aa3e41ca4810 0xa0012fdf10768f2f
    }
#+end_src

If the ~:ID:~ is missing, we assign a nil (all zero bits) OID. We reuse the same
test case file ([[ti:0001-blankline]]) from [[t:0001-blankline]].

#+name: t:0002-doc-metadata-properties-drawer-empty
#+begin_src haskell
shouldParseWithState
  "0001-blankline"
  (L.defaultOrgDoc nilOid [])
  defaultOrgParserState
#+end_src

Combine metadata together, from the top-level properties drawer and also the
key/value metadata lines.

#+name: ti:0002-doc-metadata-properties-drawer-combine
#+header: :tangle test/Lilac/ParseSpec/0002-doc-metadata-properties-drawer-combine
#+begin_src org
:PROPERTIES:
:ID:       7af1aa3e-41ca-4810-a001-2fdf10768f2f
:END:
,#+title: An Org Document
,#+author: A. U. Thor
,#+property: header-args+ :noweb no-export
#+end_src

#+name: t:0002-doc-metadata-properties-drawer-combine
#+begin_src haskell
shouldParseWithState
  "0002-doc-metadata-properties-drawer-combine"
  ( L.defaultOrgDoc
      (fromWords 0x7af1aa3e41ca4810 0xa0012fdf10768f2f)
      [ ("ID", "7af1aa3e-41ca-4810-a001-2fdf10768f2f"),
        ("author", "A. U. Thor"),
        ("property", "header-args+ :noweb no-export"),
        ("title", "An Org Document")
      ]
  )
  defaultOrgParserState
    { docOid = fromWords 0x7af1aa3e41ca4810 0xa0012fdf10768f2f
    }
#+end_src

If there is a clash, error out instead of silently choosing precedence.

#+name: ti:0002-doc-metadata-properties-drawer-clash
#+header: :tangle test/Lilac/ParseSpec/0002-doc-metadata-properties-drawer-clash
#+begin_src org
:PROPERTIES:
:ID:       00000000-0000-0000-0000-000000000000
:foo:      bar
:END:
,#+title: An Org Document
,#+author: A. U. Thor
,#+property: header-args+ :noweb no-export
,#+foo: xyz
#+end_src

#+name: t:0002-doc-metadata-properties-drawer-clash
#+begin_src haskell
before (LU.readFrom "test/Lilac/ParseSpec/" "0002-doc-metadata-properties-drawer-clash") $ do
  it "0002-doc-metadata-properties-drawer-clash" $ \input -> do
    MP.runParser
      (runStateT L.orgDocParser defaultOrgParserState)
      "0002-doc-metadata-properties-drawer-clash"
      input
      `shouldFailWith` (errFancy 181 . fancy $ MP.ErrorFail "conflicting keys in the top-level PROPERTIES drawer and document metadata key/values: [\"foo\"]")
#+end_src

* Cells
:PROPERTIES:
:ID:       ed81c307-9172-4436-8672-583ea8ecb1f9
:END:

The /cell/ is the main building block of an Org document --- it is roughly any
region of text surrounded by blank lines.

#+name: data:Cell
#+begin_src haskell
type Cell :: Type
data Cell
  = CHeading Heading
  | CListItem [ListMarker]
  | CParagraph Prose
  | CBlock Block
  | CMathFragment MathFragment
  | CFigure Figure
  | CTable Table
  | CFootnote Footnote
  | CGlossaryTerm GlossaryTerm
  | CCommand Command
  deriving stock (Eq, Ord, Generic, Show)
  deriving anyclass (FromJSON, ToJSON)
#+end_src

All of the different cell types describe some sort of content, except for one,
=CListItem=. The =CListItem= is purely structural because it only describes itself
and relies on /other cells/ for displaying any useful information. See
[[id:58f69993-599d-4389-ba47-3056b1bb2350][List items]] to see how this is used.

The [[data:Command]] encodes information about /generated/ content, such as [[id:e24de101-bf1d-418b-a18f-b1a98fea9696][Printing bibliographies]].

#+name: data:Command
#+begin_src haskell
type Command :: Type
data Command
  = PrintBibliography
  | PrintGlossary Bool
  deriving stock (Eq, Ord, Generic, Show)
  deriving anyclass (FromJSON, ToJSON)
#+end_src

The =cellParser= is responsible for parsing these different recognized values of
[[data:Cell]].

#+name: f:cellParser
#+begin_src haskell
cellParser :: OrgParser Cell
cellParser = do
  cell <-
    lexeme
      $ MP.choice
        [ toggleFootnote False $ listItemStartParser,
          toggleFootnote False $ listItemsEndParser,
          toggleFootnote False $ resetContinuationIndent headingParser,
          toggleFootnote False $ resetContinuationIndent blockParser,
          toggleFootnote False $ resetContinuationIndent figureParser,
          toggleFootnote False $ resetContinuationIndent tableParser,
          toggleFootnote False $ resetContinuationIndent mathFragmentStandaloneParser,
          toggleFootnote True $ resetContinuationIndent footnoteDefinitionParser,
          toggleFootnote False $ resetContinuationIndent bibliographyLocationParser,
          toggleFootnote False $ resetContinuationIndent glossaryLocationParser,
          toggleFootnote False $ resetContinuationIndent paragraphParser,
          detectInvalidIndentation
        ]
  modify (\s -> s {bracketBalance = 0})
  when (cell /= CListItem [])
    $ modify (\s -> s {cellNumber = s.cellNumber + 1})
  pure cell
  where
    resetContinuationIndent :: OrgParser a -> OrgParser a
    resetContinuationIndent p = do
      a <- p
      modify (\s -> s {continuationIndent = 0})
      pure a
    toggleFootnote :: Bool -> OrgParser a -> OrgParser a
    toggleFootnote b p = do
      a <- p
      modify (\s -> s {prevCellIsFootnote = b})
      pure a
#+end_src

The =resetContinuationIndent= wrapper sets the =continuationIndent= attribute in the
[[data:OrgParserState]] back to =0=; this information is useful for parsers that need
to understand indentation precisely. See [[f:listItemsEndParser]] for how it's used
by that function.

The [[f:listItemStartParser]] and [[f:listItemsEndParser]] are the only parsers that can
consume leading indentation! The [[f:paragraphParser]] can consume leading
indentation, but /only/ on lines except the first one (so that it can ensure that
paragraphs that are part of a list item can have all of its lines line up
together). For example, the following paragraph is inside a list item

  - Hello
    world.

and will be parsed as a string ~Hello world~ and not as ~Hello    world~ (the
leading indent for ~world~ will be ignored).

Any other indentation should be detected as invalid. To enforce this, we put in
a =detectInvalidIndentation= parser which technically isn't a parser but which
will throw an error if it is able to successfully parse indentation. The idea is
that if none of the other parsers in the list are able to consume indentation,
then this will allow =detectInvalidIndentation= to see such indentation and fail
the overall parser.

#+name: f:detectInvalidIndentation
#+begin_src haskell
detectInvalidIndentation :: OrgParser Cell
detectInvalidIndentation = do
  _ <- MP.hidden $ MP.takeWhile1P (Just "indentation") (== ' ')
  void
    $ MP.fancyFailure
    . Set.singleton
    . MP.ErrorFail
    $ "invalid indentation"
  pure $ CListItem []
#+end_src

We signal the error with ~invalid ...~ so as to not shadow the typical ~unexpected
...~ language from Megaparsec's usual parse failures. Note also that the last
line in the function above (~pure $ CListItem []~) is only there to satisfy the
type checker --- we will always fail if the =takeWhile1P= succeeds (the
=MP.fancyFailure= will not be executed if =takeWhile1P= fails).

The ~bracketBalance~ is set to 0 in [[f:cellParser]] after a cell is parsed, because
we're not interested in cross-cell issues about unbalanced brackets. See
[[f:failIfUnbalancedBrackets]].

* Prose
:PROPERTIES:
:ID:       c8616126-215d-49be-8874-c2aca7d93a54
:END:

Prose is a series of contiguous prose text, which can have some styling (bold,
italic, etc) as well as links, citations, footnote links, etc, or /tokens/. So
it's just a list of these /prose/ tokens (~PToken~).

#+name: newtype:Prose
#+begin_src haskell
type Prose :: Type
newtype Prose = Prose
  { unProse :: [PToken]
  }
  deriving stock (Eq, Generic, Ord, Show)
  deriving anyclass (FromJSON, ToJSON)
#+end_src

A =PToken= can be a [[id:b616c1e2-6b42-41cf-8e5d-c1832f95b59b][styled string]], [[id:f8254d58-7a3d-4595-a265-e0d6255e6ab5][link]], [[id:a54a7762-a861-42b1-b2c8-2b760281052c][citation]], or [[id:d1064535-5c98-4c80-a901-5cdc1fd5297f][math fragment]]. For the
citation, we only store the index of it (starting from 0), to act as a "pointer"
to the list of all citations in the entire document, collected in
[[f:collectCitations]].

#+name: data:PToken
#+begin_src haskell
type PToken :: Type
data PToken
  = PTokenStyledText StyledText
  | PTokenLink Link
  | PTokenCitation Int
  | PTokenMathFragment MathFragment
  | PTokenDivStart Text
  | PTokenDivEnd
  deriving stock (Eq, Generic, Ord, Show)
  deriving anyclass (FromJSON, ToJSON)
#+end_src

The ~PTokenDivStart~ and ~PTokenDivEnd~ markers are similar to the [[data:ListMarker]],
in that they encode start and end points (in HTML). Specifically, they provide
equivalent functionality of the ~CslDiv~ constructor for the ~CslJson~ type in
=Citeproc=. Arguably the ~CslDiv~ is more elegant because it encodes a recursive
list structure (by way of ~CslConcat~ and ~CslEmpty~), and also because it only
needs a single constructor ~CslDiv~ whereas we require two (~PTokenDivStart~ and
~PTokenDivEnd~). However this is the price we have to pay for not being recursive.
It also makes parsing these things difficult, so we avoid parsing them
altogether from raw text (notice how [[f:proseParser]] does not use them).

The ~proseParser~ can parse a series of [[data:PToken]] values. It is used by both
[[f:headingParser]] and [[f:paragraphParser]], because they both allow prose text in
them. In particular, it avoids parsing [[id:a54a7762-a861-42b1-b2c8-2b760281052c][Citations]] inside headings and also if
we're already inside another citation (no nesting allowed).

#+name: f:proseParser
#+begin_src haskell
proseParser :: OrgParser Text -> OrgParser Prose
proseParser spaceConsumer = do
  orgParserState <- get
  let avoidCitations =
        orgParserState.insideHeading || orgParserState.insideCitation
  a <- MP.choice $ baseParsers avoidCitations
  b <-
    MP.many
      $ MP.choice (spacePTokenParser : baseParsers avoidCitations)
  pure . Prose . filter nonEmpty $ a : b
  where
    spacePTokenParser = do
      s <- spaceConsumer
      st <- get
      pure
        $ PTokenStyledText
          StyledText {body = s, style = st.styleMask}
    baseParsers avoidCitations
      | avoidCitations =
          [ PTokenLink <$> linkParser,
            PTokenMathFragment <$> mathFragmentInlineParser,
            PTokenStyledText <$> styleMarkerParser,
            PTokenStyledText <$> styledTextParser
          ]
      | otherwise =
          [ PTokenCitation <$> citationParser,
            PTokenLink <$> linkParser,
            PTokenMathFragment <$> mathFragmentInlineParser,
            PTokenStyledText <$> styleMarkerParser,
            PTokenStyledText <$> styledTextParser
          ]
    nonEmpty :: PToken -> Bool
    nonEmpty = \case
      PTokenStyledText styledText -> not $ T.null styledText.body
      PTokenLink {}
      PTokenMathFragment {}
      PTokenCitation {}
      PTokenDivStart {}
      PTokenDivEnd {} -> True
#+end_src

In [[f:proseParser]], we parse whitespace explicitly with =whitespaceCompressor= which
consumes whitespace based on the provided =spaceConsumer=. We first parse among
the non-whitespace parsers in =baseParsers=, then parse additional tokens that may
include whitespace with =spacePTokenParser=. This way, we force the prose to
initially start out without any indentation (because the preceding cell, if any,
would have swallowed up trailing whitespace automatically via =lexeme= in
[[f:cellParser]]); otherwise, [[f:detectInvalidIndentation]] would not get a chance to
detect this (invalid) indentation.

It may appear that we could alternatively skip past spaces inside prose text
by making a =lexeme=-like wrapper which skips newlines /and spaces/ for each parser
in =baseParsers=. However, that approach has a problem. Consider the case of
parsing something like

#+begin_src org
=foo=...
#+end_src

where the ~...~ will get parsed separately from the =foo=. We'll end up with two
=PTokenStyledText= objects, one for =foo= and another for ~...~. This is a problem
because we'll also parse

#+begin_src org
=foo= ...
#+end_src

exactly the same way as two =PTokenStyledText= objects. This is because the space
between the =foo= and ~...~ will get eaten up by the wrapper /and discarded/.
And now we're in the position where we have to, on export, guess (due to
information loss) if we need to insert whitespace between the =foo= and ~...~.

Instead, explicitly parsing the whitespace with =whitespaceCompressor= lets us
parse the two cases above differently, because we save all whitespace we parsed
as their own =PTokenStyledText= objects. We call it a "compressor" because no
matter how many spaces the =spaceConsumer= consumes, we construct only 0 or 1
space characters in the =StyledText= object.

#+name: f:whitespaceCompressor
#+begin_src haskell
whitespaceCompressor :: OrgParser Text -> OrgParser Text
whitespaceCompressor spaceConsumer = do
  _ <- spaceConsumer

  MP.lookAhead
    $ MP.choice
      [ MP.eof >> pure "",
        footnoteReferenceParser >> pure "",
        MP.anySingle >> pure " "
      ]
#+end_src

This parser only succeeds if it finds at least one non-newline whitespace
character. Each time it succeeds, it "compresses" the whitespace to a single
space character --- unless it sees that the input stream ends, in which case we
use an empty string instead (0 space characters). Note that we also do a second
level of "compression" in [[f:compressProse]], where we will drop =StyledText= objects
with an empty string as the body.

Whitespace compression also happens when we detect [[id:4127feee-755d-430e-8fef-192e95143790][footnote]] references. This
way, at weaving time it can ignore any whitespace between non-whitespace text
and the footnote reference.

** Compatibility with ~CiteprocOutput~

[[https://hackage.haskell.org/package/citeproc][Citeproc]] will read the [[id:15706715-c648-4866-b757-ea4a1aad0570][CSL style file (XML)]] to understand how to make formatting
transformations (adding emphasis, changing cases, etc). The ~CiteprocOutput~
typeclass instance describes all of these [[https://github.com/jgm/citeproc/blob/8078d4c25380a37e497c7d3dc4384691be8a2b8a/src/Citeproc/Types.hs#L186-L209][these transformations]]:

#+name: CiteprocOutput typeclass
#+begin_src haskell
-- | CSL styles require certain formatting transformations to
-- be defined.  These are defined in the 'CiteprocOutput' class.
-- The library may be used with any structured format that defines
-- these operations.  See the 'Citeproc.CslJson' module for an instance
-- that corresponds to the markup allowed in CSL JSON. See
-- the 'Citeproc.Pandoc' module for an instance for Pandoc 'Inlines'.
class (Semigroup a, Monoid a, Show a, Eq a, Ord a) => CiteprocOutput a where
  toText                      :: a -> Text
  fromText                    :: Text -> a
  dropTextWhile               :: (Char -> Bool) -> a -> a
  dropTextWhileEnd            :: (Char -> Bool) -> a -> a
  addFontVariant              :: FontVariant -> a -> a
  addFontStyle                :: FontStyle -> a -> a
  addFontWeight               :: FontWeight -> a -> a
  addTextDecoration           :: TextDecoration -> a -> a
  addVerticalAlign            :: VerticalAlign -> a -> a
  addTextCase                 :: Maybe Lang -> TextCase -> a -> a
  addDisplay                  :: DisplayStyle -> a -> a
  addQuotes                   :: a -> a
  movePunctuationInsideQuotes :: a -> a
  inNote                      :: a -> a
  mapText                     :: (Text -> Text) -> a -> a
  addHyperlink                :: Text -> a -> a
  localizeQuotes              :: Locale -> a -> a
#+end_src

Any type that has an instance of the ~CiteprocOutput~ typeclass will be able to be
transformed by Citeproc according to the CSL style file. For us, this type is
[[newtype:Prose]], and if we add support for the ~CiteprocOutput~ typeclass for it,
we'll be able to format both the [[id:6fd068e3-cc95-41a4-8126-bc7bd17c79bb::data:Citation][data:Citation]] values as well as the [[id:909c5265-065a-412c-9305-2cff398ad233][CSL JSON
(bibliographical entries)]] with [[newtype:Prose]] (so that users could use the
familiar syntax for [[newtype:Prose]] in the CSL JSON directly, for example).

Unfortunately, adding in full support for all of the above functions in
[[CiteprocOutput typeclass]] is quite tedious and actually not trivial. For example
you have to know how to move around punctuation (periods and commas) from
outside of quotes (~".~) to inside (~."~). The =CslJson= type is recursive and encodes
quotes as a separate constructor, making these transformations easier.

Because of the difficulty, we don't support a full, proper implementation of
~CiteprocOutput~ for [[newtype:Prose]]; we take shortcuts and avoid doing a proper
implementation in some places (e.g., [[instance:CiteprocOutput
Prose:localizeQuotes]]). So instead of using [[newtype:Prose]], we use the ~CslJson~
type (see [[id:7874b9c5-ceac-4820-8804-7732e29f5c60::f:proseToCslJsonText][f:proseToCslJsonText]]) and convert this back into [[newtype:Prose]] (see
[[id:7874b9c5-ceac-4820-8804-7732e29f5c60::f:cslJsonTextToProse][f:cslJsonTextToProse]]).

Now you may be thinking, why bother keeping the (unfinished) implementation
of ~CiteprocOutput~ for [[newtype:Prose]]? It turns out that some of the functions
are actually quite useful, in particular [[instance:CiteprocOutput Prose:fromText]]
for implementing [[id:7874b9c5-ceac-4820-8804-7732e29f5c60::f:cslJsonTextToProse][f:cslJsonTextToProse]]. And also, we may decide to avoid this
conversion to ~CslJson~ in the future; in that scenario, having around this
unfinished implementation would provide a solid starting point.

*** ~CiteprocOutput~ typeclass implementation for [[newtype:Prose]]

~CiteprocOutput~ requires the type variable to also be an
instance of ~Semigroup~ and ~Monoid~. Because a [[newtype:Prose]] is just a wrapper
around a list of [[data:PToken]] values, we don't have to actually define anything
ourselves here (as the operations have already been defined for the list (~[]~)
type) [fn:semigroup-monoid-for-list].

#+name: instance:Semigroup Prose
#+begin_src haskell
instance Semigroup Prose where
  x <> y = Prose $ x.unProse <> y.unProse
#+end_src

#+name: instance:Monoid Prose
#+begin_src haskell
instance Monoid Prose where
  mempty = Prose []
#+end_src

We define the ~CiteprocOutput~ instance for [[newtype:Prose]] in
[[instance:CiteprocOutput Prose]] below.

#+name: instance:CiteprocOutput Prose
#+begin_src haskell
instance CiteprocOutput Prose where
  <<instance:CiteprocOutput Prose:toText>>
  <<instance:CiteprocOutput Prose:fromText>>
  dropTextWhile = dropTextFromStyledText True
  dropTextWhileEnd = dropTextFromStyledText False
  <<instance:CiteprocOutput Prose:addFontVariant>>
  <<instance:CiteprocOutput Prose:addFontStyle>>
  <<instance:CiteprocOutput Prose:addFontWeight>>
  <<instance:CiteprocOutput Prose:addTextDecoration>>
  <<instance:CiteprocOutput Prose:addVerticalAlign>>
  <<instance:CiteprocOutput Prose:addTextCase>>
  <<instance:CiteprocOutput Prose:addDisplay>>
  <<instance:CiteprocOutput Prose:addQuotes>>
  <<instance:CiteprocOutput Prose:movePunctuationInsideQuotes>>
  <<instance:CiteprocOutput Prose:inNote>>
  <<instance:CiteprocOutput Prose:mapText>>
  <<instance:CiteprocOutput Prose:addHyperlink>>
  <<instance:CiteprocOutput Prose:localizeQuotes>>
#+end_src

The functions ~toText~ and ~fromText~ are for converting the value (in this case
[[newtype:Prose]]) to and from a textual representation. For us this means,
converting [[newtype:Prose]] from text (parsing) and back to text again. Let's look
at the parsing first.

#+name: instance:CiteprocOutput Prose:fromText
#+begin_src haskell
fromText input = case MP.runParser
  (runStateT customProseParser defaultOrgParserState)
  ""
  input of
  Left err -> error . T.pack $ MP.errorBundlePretty err
  Right (prose, _) -> compressProse prose
  where
    customProseParser = do
      leadingWhitespace <- MP.optional spaceConsumer
      rest <- proseParser spaceConsumer
      case leadingWhitespace of
        Nothing -> pure rest
        Just s ->
          let w = PTokenStyledText StyledText {body = s, style = styleMaskFrom []}
           in pure . Prose $ w : rest.unProse
    spaceConsumer :: OrgParser Text
    spaceConsumer = T.pack <$> intraCellWhitespaceParser
#+end_src

This definition is mostly a wrapper around [[f:proseParser]]. The tricky part is the
leading optional whitespace. [[f:proseParser]] by default does not parse leading
whitespace, because in regular Org documents we expect indentation to have
semantic meaning for [[id:58f69993-599d-4389-ba47-3056b1bb2350][list items]]. However for citations, we don't expect to have
to deal with list items at all, so we can still parse indentation. The only
caveat is that we have to do it manually by creating a custom parser. That's
what we do with the ~customProseParser~ helper above.

The definition of ~toText~ is a bit more involved, though perhaps mechanical. The
key is to convert the [[data:PToken]] values back into the Orgmode syntax. That way
we can guarantee no loss of information going from ~Text~ to ~Prose~ and back again
to ~Text~.

#+name: instance:CiteprocOutput Prose:toText
#+begin_src haskell
toText prose = foldl' f T.empty prose.unProse
  where
    f :: Text -> PToken -> Text
    f acc = \case
      PTokenCitation {}
      PTokenDivStart {}
      PTokenDivEnd {} -> acc
      PTokenStyledText styledText
        | T.null styledText.body -> acc
        | otherwise -> acc <> unparseStyledText styledText
      PTokenLink link -> acc <> unparseLink link
      PTokenMathFragment mathFragment -> acc <> unparseMathFragment mathFragment
#+end_src

We don't support converting [[data:Citation]] back into ~Text~, because that would
suggest a nested citation (where a citation has another citation inside it),
which is strictly forbidden, as seen in [[t:0014-citations-nested]].

We also do not support converting ~PTokenDivStart~ and ~PTokenDivEnd~ to ~Text~,
because we never expect them to be written by humans; they only serve to convert
~CslJson~ in =Citeproc= back into [[newtype:Prose]]. And also, even if we supported it
here, if we wanted to round trip back from ~Text~ into [[newtype:Prose]] we would fail
because [[f:proseParser]] does not support parsing these markers.

Converting a [[data:StyledText]] back into a ~Text~ is straightforward. We simply
surround the bold text with the appropriate style markers.

#+name: f:unparseStyledText
#+begin_src haskell
unparseStyledText :: StyledText -> Text
unparseStyledText styledText =
  getStyleMarkers styledText.style
    <> escapeStyleMarkers styledText.body
    <> getStyleMarkers styledText.style
  where
    getStyleMarkers styleMask =
      foldl' accumulateStyle T.empty . sort $ maskToStyles styleMask
    accumulateStyle :: Text -> Style -> Text
    accumulateStyle acc = \case
      Bold -> acc <> "*"
      Italic -> acc <> "/"
      Underline -> acc <> "_"
      Verbatim -> acc <> "="
      Code -> acc <> "~"
      Subscript
      Superscript
      Smallcaps -> acc
    escapeStyleMarkers = T.foldl' escape T.empty
    escape acc c
      | mustEscape && c `elem` ("*/_=~" :: String) = acc <> T.pack ['\\', c]
      | otherwise = acc <> T.singleton c
    mustEscape =
      not (isCode styledText.style || isVerbatim styledText.style)
#+end_src

Converting a [[data:Link]] to ~Text~ is straightforward. We try to avoid duplicating
information (e.g., if the display name is the same as the link itself, we avoid
spelling it out).

#+name: f:unparseLink
#+begin_src haskell
unparseLink :: Link -> Text
unparseLink link = case link.target of
  LinkTargetOid linkOid ->
    let linkTargetText = show linkOid
     in if linkTargetText == displayName
          then "[[" <> linkTargetText <> "]]"
          else "[[" <> linkTargetText <> "][" <> displayName <> "]]"
  LinkTargetLineLabel name -> "[[(" <> name <> ")]]"
  LinkTargetFootnote name -> "[fn:" <> name <> "]"
  LinkTargetURI uri ->
    let linkTargetText = T.pack $ N.uriToString id uri ""
     in if linkTargetText == displayName
          then linkTargetText
          else "[[" <> linkTargetText <> "][" <> displayName <> "]]"
  LinkTargetFilePath filePath ->
    let linkTargetText = T.pack filePath
     in if linkTargetText == displayName
          then "[[file:" <> linkTargetText <> "]]"
          else "[[file:" <> linkTargetText <> "][" <> displayName <> "]]"
  where
    displayName
      | hasNoStyle link.name = T.concat $ map (\s -> s.body) link.name
      | otherwise = T.concat $ map unparseStyledText link.name
    hasNoStyle :: [StyledText] -> Bool
    hasNoStyle = null . foldl' f []
    f :: [Style] -> StyledText -> [Style]
    f acc s = acc <> maskToStyles s.style
#+end_src

For [[data:MathFragment]], we have to disambiguate the three different kinds of
declarations of math fragments --- numbered standalone
([[f:mathFragmentNumberedParser]]), unnumbered standalone
([[f:mathFragmentUnnumberedParser]]), and inline math ([[f:mathFragmentInlineParser]]).
In terms of conversion to and out of [[data:PToken]] values, however, we will only
ever use inline math, because the other ones require the math to be in its own
cell as a ~CMathFragment~ at the [[data:Cell]] level, outside of a [[newtype:Prose]].
Still, we check for all three types of math fragments for completeness.

#+name: f:unparseMathFragment
#+begin_src haskell
unparseMathFragment :: MathFragment -> Text
unparseMathFragment mathFragment = mathStart <> mathFragment.body <> mathEnd
  where
    (mathStart, mathEnd)
      | Just _ <- lookup "__equation_number" mathFragment.meta =
          ("\\begin{equation}", "\\end{equation}")
      | Just _ <- lookup "__equation_anon_number" mathFragment.meta =
          ("\\[", "\\]")
      | otherwise = ("\\(", "\\)")
#+end_src

Now we come to ~dropTextWhile~ and ~dropTextWhileEnd~ functions for the typeclass.
These functions are for deleting ~Text~, and correspond to ~T.dropWhile~ and
~T.dropWhileEnd~, respectively.

Let's consider an example of how these functions might be implemented. If you
look at the [[https://github.com/jgm/citeproc/blob/f0b55e2a8363f5c80d5d63638696e475e524b340/src/Citeproc/CslJson.hs#L155-L184][definitions used for the ~CslJson~ type in Citeproc]], you can see that
they assume the encoded value is just some ~Text~ values (inside the ~CslText~
constructor). This makes sense for the ~CslJson~ type because it only encodes a
~Text~ value, surrounded by any number of other constructors that encode things
like emphasis (e.g., ~CslBold~).

Our [[newtype:Prose]] is a bit different because it has more semantic information
encoded in it beyond just raw ~Text~ (see [[data:PToken]]). The only really equivalent
value is the ~PTokenStyledText~ constructor which only deals with ~Text~ with some
styling on top. The other things like ~PTokenLink~ and ~PTokenMathFragment~ are not
really ~Text~ values (we ignore ~PTokenCitation~ because citations cannot be
nested). So running ~T.dropWhile~ won't make much sense if we're looking at a ~PTokenMathFragment~.

Because of the above issue, we compromise by skipping over any
non-~PTokenStyledText~ value.

#+name: f:dropTextFromStyledText
#+begin_src haskell
dropTextFromStyledText :: Bool -> (Char -> Bool) -> Prose -> Prose
dropTextFromStyledText dropFromStart f prose =
  Prose
    . (if dropFromStart then reverse else id)
    . fst
    . foldl' g ([], False)
    $ if dropFromStart then prose.unProse else reverse prose.unProse
  where
    g :: ([PToken], Bool) -> PToken -> ([PToken], Bool)
    g (acc, pastFirst) pToken
      | pastFirst = (pToken : acc, pastFirst)
      | otherwise = case pToken of
          PTokenStyledText styledText
            | T.null styledText.body -> (pToken : acc, pastFirst)
            | otherwise ->
                let modifiedBody = dropper f styledText.body
                    modified =
                      PTokenStyledText
                        StyledText
                          { body = modifiedBody,
                            style = styledText.style
                          }
                 in if T.null modifiedBody
                      then (acc, True)
                      else (modified : acc, True)
          PTokenLink {}
          PTokenMathFragment {}
          PTokenCitation {}
          PTokenDivStart {}
          PTokenDivEnd {} -> (pToken : acc, pastFirst)
    dropper = if dropFromStart then T.dropWhile else T.dropWhileEnd
#+end_src

Adding font variations is not supported, because we have no notion of small caps
as a stylistic option.

#+name: instance:CiteprocOutput Prose:addFontVariant
#+begin_src haskell
addFontVariant fontVariant prose = case fontVariant of
  NormalVariant -> prose
  SmallCapsVariant -> prose
#+end_src

For font styles, we treat ~ObliqueFont~ the same as ~ItalicFont~. For ~NormalFont~
though, we simply strip all styling.

#+name: instance:CiteprocOutput Prose:addFontStyle
#+begin_src haskell
addFontStyle fontStyle prose = case fontStyle of
  NormalFont -> Prose $ map noStyle prose.unProse
  ItalicFont -> Prose $ map italicize prose.unProse
  ObliqueFont -> Prose $ map italicize prose.unProse
  where
    noStyle pToken = case pToken of
      PTokenStyledText styledText ->
        PTokenStyledText
          StyledText
            { body = styledText.body,
              style = styleMaskFrom []
            }
      PTokenLink {}
      PTokenCitation {}
      PTokenMathFragment {}
      PTokenDivStart {}
      PTokenDivEnd {} -> pToken
    italicize pToken = case pToken of
      PTokenStyledText styledText ->
        PTokenStyledText
          StyledText
            { body = styledText.body,
              style = styleMaskFrom $ Italic : maskToStyles styledText.style
            }
      PTokenLink {}
      PTokenCitation {}
      PTokenMathFragment {}
      PTokenDivStart {}
      PTokenDivEnd {} -> pToken
#+end_src

For the font weight, we only have one weight, which is ~Bold~.

#+name: instance:CiteprocOutput Prose:addFontWeight
#+begin_src haskell
addFontWeight fontWeight prose = case fontWeight of
  NormalWeight -> Prose $ map unBold prose.unProse
  BoldWeight -> Prose $ map bold prose.unProse
  LightWeight -> Prose $ map unBold prose.unProse
  where
    bold pToken = case pToken of
      PTokenStyledText styledText ->
        PTokenStyledText
          StyledText
            { body = styledText.body,
              style = styleMaskFrom $ Bold : maskToStyles styledText.style
            }
      PTokenLink {}
      PTokenCitation {}
      PTokenMathFragment {}
      PTokenDivStart {}
      PTokenDivEnd {} -> pToken
    unBold pToken = case pToken of
      PTokenStyledText styledText ->
        PTokenStyledText
          StyledText
            { body = styledText.body,
              style =
                styleMaskFrom
                  . filter (/= Bold)
                  $ maskToStyles styledText.style
            }
      PTokenLink {}
      PTokenCitation {}
      PTokenMathFragment {}
      PTokenDivStart {}
      PTokenDivEnd {} -> pToken
#+end_src

The ~addTextDecoration~ definition is straightforward enough, because there is no
information loss (it's either having an underline or not having one).

#+name: instance:CiteprocOutput Prose:addTextDecoration
#+begin_src haskell
addTextDecoration textDecoration prose = case textDecoration of
  NoDecoration -> Prose $ map noUnderline prose.unProse
  UnderlineDecoration -> Prose $ map underline prose.unProse
  where
    underline pToken = case pToken of
      PTokenStyledText styledText ->
        PTokenStyledText
          StyledText
            { body = styledText.body,
              style = styleMaskFrom $ Underline : maskToStyles styledText.style
            }
      PTokenLink {}
      PTokenCitation {}
      PTokenMathFragment {}
      PTokenDivStart {}
      PTokenDivEnd {} -> pToken
    noUnderline pToken = case pToken of
      PTokenStyledText styledText ->
        PTokenStyledText
          StyledText
            { body = styledText.body,
              style =
                styleMaskFrom
                  . filter (/= Underline)
                  $ maskToStyles styledText.style
            }
      PTokenLink {}
      PTokenCitation {}
      PTokenMathFragment {}
      PTokenDivStart {}
      PTokenDivEnd {} -> pToken
#+end_src

We don't support adding vertical alignment, so we don't do anything here.

#+name: instance:CiteprocOutput Prose:addVerticalAlign
#+begin_src haskell
addVerticalAlign verticalAlign prose = case verticalAlign of
  BaselineAlign
  SupAlign
  SubAlign -> prose
#+end_src

We don't support case transformations (converting to uppercase, lowercase, etc).
The =Citeproc= library has a =Citeproc.CaseTransform= module which provides much of
the work for this. However, we don't bother with this for now to avoid
additional complexity.

#+name: instance:CiteprocOutput Prose:addTextCase
#+begin_src haskell
addTextCase _ textCase prose = case textCase of
  Lowercase
  Uppercase
  CapitalizeFirst
  CapitalizeAll
  SentenceCase
  TitleCase
  PreserveCase -> prose
#+end_src

We don't support display transformations (whether to display as a block,
indented, etc). This is presumably for the bibliography list only.

#+name: instance:CiteprocOutput Prose:addDisplay
#+begin_src haskell
addDisplay displayStyle prose = case displayStyle of
  DisplayBlock
  DisplayLeftMargin
  DisplayRightInline
  DisplayIndent -> prose
#+end_src

Adding quotes means just surrounding the prose with quote characters.

#+name: instance:CiteprocOutput Prose:addQuotes
#+begin_src haskell
addQuotes prose = Prose $ [quote] <> prose.unProse <> [quote]
  where
    quote =
      PTokenStyledText
        StyledText
          { body = "\"",
            style = styleMaskFrom []
          }
#+end_src

The ~movePunctuationInsideQuotes~ function moves all commas and periods to come
just /before/ the quote, instead of after it. For example, ~"a".~ would be converted
into ~"b."~. Because of the way styles are handled though, a [[data:PToken]] can only
see text that have the same style; if there are two of them, and they each have
a piece of the punctuation combination we are concerned about, this won't work.
However, such cases should be rare, and even if we did support that case, it
wouldn't make sense to move a bold period inside of a non-bold quote character,
for example. So working inside of a single [[data:PToken]] should be reasonable
enough.

#+name: instance:CiteprocOutput Prose:movePunctuationInsideQuotes
#+begin_src haskell
movePunctuationInsideQuotes prose =
  Prose $ map f prose.unProse
  where
    f pToken = case pToken of
      PTokenStyledText styledText ->
        PTokenStyledText
          StyledText
            { body = g styledText.body,
              style = styledText.style
            }
      PTokenLink {}
      PTokenCitation {}
      PTokenMathFragment {}
      PTokenDivStart {}
      PTokenDivEnd {} -> pToken
    g s =
      let attempts = map (tryRearrange s) punctCombos
       in case find (\(mightHaveMore, _) -> mightHaveMore) attempts of
            Just (_, s') -> g s'
            Nothing -> s
    tryRearrange s punctCombo
      | (prefix, remainder) <- T.breakOn punctCombo s,
        not (T.null remainder) =
          (True, prefix <> T.reverse punctCombo <> T.drop (T.length punctCombo) remainder)
      | otherwise = (False, s)
    punctCombos :: [Text]
    punctCombos =
      [ "\".",
        "\",",
        "'.",
        "',"
      ]
#+end_src

We don't support ~inNote~. [[https://github.com/jgm/citeproc/blob/f0b55e2a8363f5c80d5d63638696e475e524b340/src/Citeproc/CslJson.hs#L148][CslJson]] doesn't support it either.

#+name: instance:CiteprocOutput Prose:inNote
#+begin_src haskell
inNote = id
#+end_src

~mapText~ just applies an arbitrary function to all ~Text~ values.

#+name: instance:CiteprocOutput Prose:mapText
#+begin_src haskell
mapText f prose = Prose $ map g prose.unProse
  where
    g :: PToken -> PToken
    g pToken = case pToken of
      PTokenStyledText styledText ->
        PTokenStyledText
          StyledText
            { body = f styledText.body,
              style = styledText.style
            }
      PTokenLink {}
      PTokenCitation {}
      PTokenMathFragment {}
      PTokenDivStart {}
      PTokenDivEnd {} -> pToken
#+end_src

~addHyperlink~ just adds a URL; we have a rich [[data:Link]] type for this, so that's
what we use.

#+name: instance:CiteprocOutput Prose:addHyperlink
#+begin_src haskell
addHyperlink url prose = Prose $ maybeToList link <> prose.unProse
  where
    link = case N.parseURI $ T.unpack url of
      Nothing -> Nothing
      Just uri ->
        Just
          $ PTokenLink
            Link
              { name =
                  [ StyledText
                      { body = url,
                        style = styleMaskFrom []
                      }
                  ],
                target = LinkTargetURI uri
              }
#+end_src

We don't support localization of quotes.

#+name: instance:CiteprocOutput Prose:localizeQuotes
#+begin_src haskell
localizeQuotes _ prose = prose
#+end_src

** Tests

We need to check that there is no information loss going the full round trip
from ~Text~ to ~Prose~ and back to ~Text~ again.

#+name: f:checkTextToProseRoundTrip
#+begin_src haskell
checkTextToProseRoundTrip :: String -> [(Text, Prose)] -> Spec
checkTextToProseRoundTrip msg testCases = do
  it msg $ do
    forM_ testCases $ \(text, prose) -> do
      CP.fromText text `shouldBe` prose
      CP.toText prose `shouldBe` text
#+end_src

#+name: Text to Prose round trip
#+begin_src haskell
describe "Text to Prose round trip" $ do
  checkTextToProseRoundTrip
    "can handle StyledText"
    [ ( "no style",
        Prose [ptext "no style" []]
      ),
      ( "*bold*",
        Prose [ptext "bold" [Bold]]
      ),
      ( "*bo\\*ld (escaped)*",
        Prose [ptext "bo*ld (escaped)" [Bold]]
      ),
      ( "/italic/",
        Prose [ptext "italic" [Italic]]
      ),
      ( "/it\\/alic (escaped)/",
        Prose [ptext "it/alic (escaped)" [Italic]]
      ),
      ( "_underline_",
        Prose [ptext "underline" [Underline]]
      ),
      ( "_under\\_line (escaped)_",
        Prose [ptext "under_line (escaped)" [Underline]]
      ),
      ( "=*/_~verbatim=",
        Prose [ptext "*/_~verbatim" [Verbatim]]
      ),
      ( "~*/_=code~",
        Prose [ptext "*/_=code" [Code]]
      )
    ]
  checkTextToProseRoundTrip
    "can handle OID links"
    [ ( "[[id:aaaaaaaa-aaaa-aaaa-aaaa-aaaaaaaaaaa1]]",
        Prose
          [ PTokenLink
              Link
                { name = [stext "id:aaaaaaaa-aaaa-aaaa-aaaa-aaaaaaaaaaa1" []],
                  target =
                    LinkTargetOid
                      LinkOid
                        { oid = fromWords 0xaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaa 0xaaaaaaaaaaaaaaa1,
                          name = ""
                        }
                }
          ]
      ),
      ( "[[id:aaaaaaaa-aaaa-aaaa-aaaa-aaaaaaaaaaa2][*foo*]]",
        Prose
          [ PTokenLink
              Link
                { name = [stext "foo" [Bold]],
                  target =
                    LinkTargetOid
                      LinkOid
                        { oid = fromWords 0xaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaa 0xaaaaaaaaaaaaaaa2,
                          name = ""
                        }
                }
          ]
      ),
      ( "[[id:aaaaaaaa-aaaa-aaaa-aaaa-aaaaaaaaaaa3::abc][/bar/]]",
        Prose
          [ PTokenLink
              Link
                { name = [stext "bar" [Italic]],
                  target =
                    LinkTargetOid
                      LinkOid
                        { oid = fromWords 0xaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaa 0xaaaaaaaaaaaaaaa3,
                          name = "abc"
                        }
                }
          ]
      )
    ]
  checkTextToProseRoundTrip
    "can handle footnote links"
    [ ( "[fn:abc]",
        Prose
          [ PTokenLink
              Link
                { name = [stext "1" []],
                  target = LinkTargetFootnote "abc"
                }
          ]
      ),
      ( "[fn:abc][fn:xyz][fn:abc]",
        Prose
          [ PTokenLink
              Link
                { name = [stext "1" []],
                  target = LinkTargetFootnote "abc"
                },
            PTokenLink
              Link
                { name = [stext "2" []],
                  target = LinkTargetFootnote "xyz"
                },
            PTokenLink
              Link
                { name = [stext "1" []],
                  target = LinkTargetFootnote "abc"
                }
          ]
      )
    ]
  checkTextToProseRoundTrip
    "can handle web links"
    [ ( "[[https://foo.org][bar]]",
        Prose
          [ PTokenLink
              Link
                { name = [stext "bar" []],
                  target =
                    LinkTargetURI
                      N.URI
                        { N.uriScheme = "https:",
                          N.uriAuthority =
                            Just
                              N.URIAuth
                                { N.uriUserInfo = "",
                                  N.uriRegName = "foo.org",
                                  N.uriPort = ""
                                },
                          N.uriPath = "",
                          N.uriQuery = "",
                          N.uriFragment = ""
                        }
                }
          ]
      ),
      ( "https://foo2.org",
        Prose
          [ PTokenLink
              Link
                { name = [stext "https://foo2.org" []],
                  target =
                    LinkTargetURI
                      N.URI
                        { N.uriScheme = "https:",
                          N.uriAuthority =
                            Just
                              N.URIAuth
                                { N.uriUserInfo = "",
                                  N.uriRegName = "foo2.org",
                                  N.uriPort = ""
                                },
                          N.uriPath = "",
                          N.uriQuery = "",
                          N.uriFragment = ""
                        }
                }
          ]
      )
    ]
  checkTextToProseRoundTrip
    "can handle file links"
    [ ( "[[file:foo/bar]]",
        Prose
          [ PTokenLink
              Link
                { name = [stext "foo/bar" []],
                  target = LinkTargetFilePath "foo/bar"
                }
          ]
      ),
      ( "[[file:foo/bar][baz]]",
        Prose
          [ PTokenLink
              Link
                { name = [stext "baz" []],
                  target = LinkTargetFilePath "foo/bar"
                }
          ]
      )
    ]
  checkTextToProseRoundTrip
    "can handle inline math fragments"
    [ ( "\\(4 = 2 * 2\\)",
        Prose
          [ PTokenMathFragment
              MathFragment
                { parentOid = nilOid,
                  body = "4 = 2 * 2",
                  meta = fromList []
                }
          ]
      )
    ]
#+end_src

We can't check for non-inline math fragments in the test above, because
[[f:proseParser]] only allows for inline math fragments.

Check we can drop text characters from either end of [[newtype:Prose]].

#+name: Drop text chars from Prose
#+begin_src haskell
describe "Drop text chars from Prose" $ do
  it "can handle plain text from the front" $ do
    CP.dropTextWhile (== 'a') (CP.fromText "aa b *cc*" :: Prose)
      `shouldBe` (CP.fromText " b *cc*")
  it "can handle plain text from the back" $ do
    CP.dropTextWhileEnd (== 'c') (CP.fromText "aa b *cc*" :: Prose)
      `shouldBe` (CP.fromText "aa b ")
  it "can handle styled (italic) text from the front" $ do
    CP.dropTextWhile (== 'a') (CP.fromText "/aa/ b *cc*" :: Prose)
      `shouldBe` (CP.fromText " b *cc*")
  it "can handle styled (italic) text from the back" $ do
    CP.dropTextWhileEnd (== 'c') (CP.fromText "/aa/ b *cc*" :: Prose)
      `shouldBe` (CP.fromText "/aa/ b ")
  it "skips over non-text elements from the front" $ do
    CP.dropTextWhile (== 'a') (CP.fromText "[[foo]]/aa/ b *cc*" :: Prose)
      `shouldBe` (CP.fromText "[[foo]] b *cc*")
  it "skips over non-text elements from the back" $ do
    CP.dropTextWhileEnd (== 'c') (CP.fromText "/aa/ b *cc*[[foo]]" :: Prose)
      `shouldBe` (CP.fromText "/aa/ b [[foo]]")
#+end_src

* Styled strings
:PROPERTIES:
:ID:       b616c1e2-6b42-41cf-8e5d-c1832f95b59b
:END:

A =StyledText= is just some text that has one or more styles attached to it.  Here
are some examples: *bold*, /italic/, _underline_, =verbatim=, and ~code~.

#+name: data:StyledText
#+begin_src haskell
type StyledText :: Type
data StyledText = StyledText
  { body :: Text,
    style :: StyleMask
  }
  deriving stock (Eq, Generic, Ord, Show)
  deriving anyclass (FromJSON, ToJSON)
#+end_src

The [[newtype:StyleMask]] is a low-level bit mask that can store multiple boolean
values of each of the supported [[data:Style]] bits. If the string has no style,
then it's not stylized at all and the =StyleMask= will be 0 (no bits set). See
[[id:87cd7d62-1f5c-4442-87ff-e1f2a68a8e56][=StyleMask= details]] for a discussion of how this data structure is used and
transformed into a list of =Style= values. For now, we can just pretend that the
=StyleMask= is like a list of [[data:Style]] values.

In order to save some keystrokes, we use a couple functions, [[f:stext]] and
[[f:ptext]].

#+name: f:stext
#+begin_src haskell
stext :: Text -> [Style] -> StyledText
stext body styles = StyledText {body = body, style = styleMaskFrom styles}
#+end_src

#+name: f:ptext
#+begin_src haskell
ptext :: Text -> [Style] -> PToken
ptext body styles = PTokenStyledText $ stext body styles
#+end_src

The [[data:Style]] type is straightforward enough. For now we don't support all
possible styles supported by Orgmode (such as strike-through text), for both
simplicity but also prescriptive reasons. For example, strike-through text
should never be needed during exposition.

#+name: data:Style
#+begin_src haskell
type Style :: Type
data Style
  = Bold
  | Italic
  | Underline
  | Verbatim
  | Code
