  | Smallcaps
  | Subscript
  | Superscript
  deriving stock (Enum, Eq, Generic, Ord, Show)
  deriving anyclass (FromJSON, ToJSON)
#+end_src

The =styleMarkers= keeps track of the characters that activate and deactivate each
style. It has the syntactic characters we use to /mark/ text with the a supported
/style/. Hence the term /style markers/.

We don't actually allow users to use the ~Smallcaps~, ~Subscript~, or ~Superscript~
styles themselves (this is why they're missing from [[f:styleMarkers]]); these are
used inside [[id:7874b9c5-ceac-4820-8804-7732e29f5c60::f:cslJsonTextToProse][f:cslJsonTextToProse]] and [[id:d6abea81-d620-4ede-9c2c-68f7ea07f847::f:weaveStyledText][f:weaveStyledText]] to preserve these styles
that ~CslJson~ might generate for citation processing.

#+name: f:styleMarkers
#+begin_src haskell
styleMarkers :: Map Char Style
styleMarkers =
  fromList
    [ ('*', Bold),
      ('/', Italic),
      ('_', Underline),
      ('=', Verbatim),
      ('~', Code)
    ]
#+end_src

Characters in the =styleMarkers= can be escaped with a backslash. For example, the
strings ~1\*2~ and ~a\_b~ are interpreted as ~1*2~ and ~a_b~, because they act to
escape the style markers ~*~ and ~_~, respectively. If you want to write a literal
backslash, you must use ~\\~. This use of the backslash as an escape character
deviates from Orgmode.

This rule also applies for slashes, such as ~1\/2~ in
[[ti:0005-styled-strings-escape-marker]]. However, for slashes there is a simpler
(and arguably more intuitive syntax) where slashes inside a word are not treated
as style markers, so they don't need to be escaped. See [[f:slashSeparatedWords]]
and [[ti:0005-styled-strings-slash-separated-words]].

#+name: ti:0005-styled-strings-escape-marker
#+header: :tangle test/Lilac/ParseSpec/0005-styled-strings-escape-marker
#+begin_src org
/foo and\/or 1\/2 bar \\ a\\b/
#+end_src

#+name: t:0005-styled-strings-escape-marker
#+begin_src haskell
shouldParseWith
  "0005-styled-strings-escape-marker"
  $ nilOrgDoc
    [ CParagraph
        $ Prose
          <<Expected prose for 0005-styled-strings-escape-marker>>
    ]
    []
#+end_src

#+name: Expected prose for 0005-styled-strings-escape-marker
#+begin_src haskell
[ ptext "foo and/or 1/2 bar \\ a\\b" [Italic]
]
#+end_src

** Parsing

Parsing styled strings is somewhat tricky, because of two main problems:

  1) the markers use the same character for both activation and deactivation,
     and

  2) the styles can be nested or interleaved.

You can have, for example, lines that are /italicized like this but also have
*bold text inside* of it/.

We approach these problems with a simple idea: use a *bit mask*
([[newtype:StyleMask]]) to keep track of markers. Every time we see a starting
marker, we turn on the bit in the mask to activate that style (if it's off), or
turn it off (if it's on), by calling [[f:styleMaskFlip]].

If the input is such that the markers are not balanced, such that by the time we
finish parsing a paragraph (or end of a heading) and the mask still has active
styles in it, we throw an error with [[f:failIfLeftoverActiveStyles]]. That is, we
expect style starting and ending markers to always be balanced by the end of
every paragraph or heading. This deviates from vanilla Org, which does not have
this restriction.

#+name: f:failIfLeftoverActiveStyles
#+begin_src haskell
failIfLeftoverActiveStyles :: OrgParser ()
failIfLeftoverActiveStyles = do
  orgParserState <- get
  let (StyleMask mask) = orgParserState.styleMask
  when (mask /= 0) $ do
    fail
      $ "leftover active styles: "
      <> (show $ maskToStyles $ StyleMask mask)
#+end_src

We don't really care which styles are turned on or off at which point (the style
markers can be used at the beginning, middle, or end of a word --- where a word
is any sequence of non-whitespace characters). The only thing we check is that
by the end of the cell (heading or paragraph), all styles must be turned off.

#+name: ti:0005-styled-strings-messy-boundaries
#+header: :tangle test/Lilac/ParseSpec/0005-styled-strings-messy-boundaries
#+begin_src org
,* *foo *bar baz a*b*c (*ok*) **yes
#+end_src

#+name: t:0005-styled-strings-messy-boundaries
#+begin_src haskell
shouldParseWithState
  "0005-styled-strings-messy-boundaries"
  ( nilOrgDoc
      [ CHeading
          Heading
            { level = 1,
              section = [1],
              body =
                Prose
                  <<Expected prose for 0005-styled-strings-messy-boundaries>>,
              meta = fromList []
            }
      ]
      []
  )
  defaultOrgParserState
    { headingOidStack = [Nothing],
      headingLevel = 1,
      headingSectionNumber = [1]
    }
#+end_src

#+name: Expected prose for 0005-styled-strings-messy-boundaries
#+begin_src haskell
[ ptext "foo " [Bold],
  ptext "bar baz a" [],
  ptext "b" [Bold],
  ptext "c (" [],
  ptext "ok" [Bold],
  ptext ") yes" []
]
#+end_src

In [[ti:0005-styled-strings-leftover-active-styles]] we keep parsing to the end of
the heading, expecting to see the ending style marker for bold text, but never
see it. So the failure from [[f:failIfLeftoverActiveStyles]] gets triggered.

#+name: ti:0005-styled-strings-leftover-active-styles
#+header: :tangle test/Lilac/ParseSpec/0005-styled-strings-leftover-active-styles
#+begin_src org
,* foo *bar baz
#+end_src

#+name: t:0005-styled-strings-leftover-active-styles
#+begin_src haskell
shouldFailWithMessage
  "0005-styled-strings-leftover-active-styles"
  "leftover active styles: [Bold]"
  15
#+end_src

Note that we don't wait until seeing the closing marker to see if we should
stylize the text at hand; the =StyleMask= is "strict" in this sense. This way, we
can start parsing stylized text without needing to see a corresponding ending
marker. This imperative style is easier to think about, and is one of the
reasons why we decided to go with having the =StyleMask= in the
[[data:OrgParserState]].

The algorithm for parsing [[data:StyledText]] requires parsing style markers (and
only style markers), or parsing other text that will be affected by style
markers. Let's first look at the simpler case, that of parsing style markers.

#+name: f:styleMarkerParser
#+begin_src haskell
styleMarkerParser :: OrgParser StyledText
styleMarkerParser = do
  orgParserState <- get

  let mask = orgParserState.styleMask
  c <- MP.satisfy $ isStyleMarker mask
  styleMask <- toStyleMask c

  let newMask = styleMaskFlip mask styleMask

  put orgParserState {styleMask = newMask}
  pure
    StyledText
      { body = "",
        style = newMask
      }
#+end_src

All we do is parse a single style marker character, and flip the bit for it as
either on or off, with [[f:styleMaskFlip]]. Ideally we wouldn't even bother
returning anything at the end of [[f:styleMarkerParser]] (because we've updated the
~orgParserState~ and that's the only essential thing we must do). But because of
the way it is used in [[f:proseParser]], we have to return a ~StyledText~. We return
an empty ~body~ field though, so that we can filter it out in the ~nonEmpty~ helper
in [[f:proseParser]].

We cannot parse multiple style markers in one go, because [[f:isStyleMarker]]
changes behavior depending on the current active style mask. If we are in a
verbatim or code style, we ignore all other style markers except for those that
will end the current (verbatim or code) style. Otherwise, we won't be able to
write stuff like ~*~ because we would think that the asterisk in there is starting
a bold style (which it is not because it is surrounded by =~= to mark code).

#+name: f:isStyleMarker
#+begin_src haskell
isStyleMarker :: StyleMask -> Char -> Bool
isStyleMarker styleMask c
  | isVerbatim styleMask = c == '='
  | isCode styleMask = c == '~'
  | otherwise = member c styleMarkers
#+end_src

Parsing actual styled text requires reading the state of the =StyleMask=, and then
reading some text. This is done in [[f:styledTextParser]]. It delegates essentially
all of the work to [[f:textParser]].

#+name: f:styledTextParser
#+begin_src haskell
styledTextParser :: OrgParser StyledText
styledTextParser = do
  orgParserState <- get

  let mask = orgParserState.styleMask

  text <- textParser mask

  pure
    StyledText
      { body = text,
        style = mask
      }
#+end_src

The [[f:textParser]] is careful not to step on other parsers' toes, because in a
sense it is the most "greedy" parser, as it can parse many different types of
input.

#+name: f:textParser
#+begin_src haskell
textParser :: StyleMask -> OrgParser Text
textParser styleMask
  | isVerbatim styleMask =
      MP.takeWhile1P (Just "verbatim text") isNotVerbatimMarker
  | isCode styleMask =
      MP.takeWhile1P (Just "code") isNotCodeMarker
  | otherwise = do
      o <- MP.getOffset
      word <-
        MP.choice
          [ MP.try $ slashSeparatedWords,
            MP.label "normal characters" normalChars,
            escapedSymbolParser styleMask
          ]
      orgParserState <- get
      let haveNotSeenGlossaryTermDelimiterYet =
            orgParserState.insideParagraph && orgParserState.glossaryTermOffset == 0
          wordIsGlossaryTermDelimiter = word == "::" && isEmptyMask styleMask

      when (haveNotSeenGlossaryTermDelimiterYet && wordIsGlossaryTermDelimiter) $ do
        modify (\s -> s {glossaryTermOffset = o}) -- (ref:glossary-term-delimiter-detection)
      pure word
  where
    isNotVerbatimMarker c = c /= '='
    isNotCodeMarker c = c /= '~'
    normalChars :: OrgParser Text
    normalChars = do
      cs <- MP.some $ normalChar
      pure $ T.pack cs
    normalChar :: OrgParser Char
    normalChar = do
      orgParserState <- get
      let toAvoid =
            [ (orgParserState.insideCitation, string ";"),
              (orgParserState.insideCitePrefix, citeIdParser),
              ( orgParserState.insideCitation
                  && (orgParserState.bracketBalance == 0),
                string "]"
              ),
              (orgParserState.insideLinkDisplayName, string "]]"),
              ( not orgParserState.insideCitation
                  && not orgParserState.insideCitePrefix
                  && not orgParserState.insideLinkDisplayName,
                string "[["
              )
            ]
      forM_
        toAvoid
        $ \(condition, p) -> when condition $ MP.notFollowedBy p
      c <- MP.satisfy isNormalChar
      let bracketBalanceDiff
            | c == '[' = 1
            | c == ']' = -1
            | otherwise = 0
      modify (\s -> s {bracketBalance = s.bracketBalance + bracketBalanceDiff})
      pure c
    isNormalChar c =
      notMember c styleMarkers
        && (not $ isSpace c)
        && (not $ c == '\\')
#+end_src

Because of the way =isStyleMarker= and =isProseWord= checks membership of the
characters used for activating (and deactivating) styles in [[f:styleMarkers]], we
take special care to escape these characters with [[f:escapedSymbolParser]]. If the
style markers are not escaped, using any of them will immediately try to
activate or deactivate a style (modulo the special consideration for verbatim
and code markers as discussed in [[f:isStyleMarker]], but also in
[[f:slashSeparatedWords]]).

[[f:escapedSymbolParser]] also lets users insert a newline character with ~\n~. This
way, you can write multiline text inside a table, even if the table is not
taking up the full width of the HTML page (at which point the browser will
insert soft newlines on its own).

#+name: f:escapedSymbolParser
#+begin_src haskell
escapedSymbolParser :: StyleMask -> OrgParser Text
escapedSymbolParser styleMask = do
  _ <- char '\\'
  s <-
    MP.choice
      [ do
          c <- MP.satisfy (\c -> isStyleMarker styleMask c || c == '\\')
          pure $ T.singleton c,
        string "n",
        string "vert{}"
      ]
  pure $ case s of
    "vert{}" -> "|"
    "n" -> "\n"
    _ -> s
#+end_src

The word =\vert{}= can be used to escape a pipe character inside [[data:Table][table]] cells,
because although our Megaparsec-powered parser is powerful enough to handle
escaping pipe characters with =\|= (for consistency with =\/= to escape the italic
style marker), Orgmode is unable to deal with this in Emacs (so pressing ~TAB~
will mess up the alignment of table cells). For this reason, we use =\vert{}=
instead. Of course, outside of tables the pipe character can be written raw like
this "|" without issue.

There is one exception to this, and it's what we call "slash-separated words".
These are cases where we see one or more slash characters inside an alphanumeric
word, such as and/or, or a plain (non-math) fraction like 1/2, or even something
like 1/2/3. In these cases, we don't want the slashes to be treated as italic
style markers.

#+name: f:slashSeparatedWords
#+begin_src haskell
slashSeparatedWords :: OrgParser Text
slashSeparatedWords = do
  let wordParser :: OrgParser Text
      wordParser = do
        _ <- cannotStartWithClosingPunc
        MP.takeWhile1P (Just "slash-separated words") isWordChar
  firstWord <- wordParser
  remainingWords <-
    MP.some $ do
      _ <- MP.notFollowedBy $ MP.choice [string "/ ", string "/\n"]
      _ <- char '/'
      word <- wordParser
      pure $ "/" <> word
  when (lastWordIsMeaningless $ reverse remainingWords)
    $ fail "last word is not meaningful; unlikely to be intended as a slash-separated-word"
  let allWords = firstWord : remainingWords
  when (hasLinkDelimiter allWords)
    $ fail "has link delimiter \"[[\"; unlikely to be intended as a slash-separated-word"
  pure $ T.concat allWords
  where
    cannotStartWithClosingPunc :: OrgParser ()
    cannotStartWithClosingPunc =
      MP.notFollowedBy
        $ MP.choice
          [ string "]",
            string "}",
            string ")",
            string "]]"
          ]
    isWordChar c = not (isSpace c) && c `notElem` (keys styleMarkers <> "\\")
    lastWordIsMeaningless :: [Text] -> Bool
    lastWordIsMeaningless = \case
      [] -> True
      (lastWord : [])
        | lastWord `elem` ["/.", "/,", "/:"] -> True
        | otherwise -> False
      _ -> False
    hasLinkDelimiter :: [Text] -> Bool
    hasLinkDelimiter = or . map ("[[" `T.isInfixOf`)
#+end_src

The rules inside [[f:slashSeparatedWords]] are a "best effort" attempt, not
something that is always correct. For the cases where we need more flexibility,
you can use escaped slashes like in
[[ti:0005-styled-strings-escape-marker]].

See [[ti:0005-styled-strings-slash-separated-words]] for some real-world examples.

** =StyleMask= details
:PROPERTIES:
:ID:       87cd7d62-1f5c-4442-87ff-e1f2a68a8e56
:END:

The previous section explained how we parse styled strings without getting into
the implementation behind the =StyleMask=. We now get into these implementation
details.

The =StyleMask= is an 8-bit word, which can hold boolean values for the 6
different =Style= data constructors, e.g. [[data:Style][Bold, Italic, etc]]. It's a
space-efficient type to replace an equivalent map or list of =[Style]= values. We
use a =newtype= around a =Word8= (an 8-bit word) so as to keep it distinct from
=Word8= itself.

#+name: newtype:StyleMask
#+begin_src haskell
type StyleMask :: Type
newtype StyleMask = StyleMask Word8
  deriving stock (Eq, Generic, Ord)
  deriving anyclass (FromJSON, ToJSON)
#+end_src

We have a custom =Show= instance because the default =Show= would just treat the
=Word8= as a number. Instead we want to show what the binary bits (turned on
inside the =Word8=) would represent in terms of actual styles. We have to import
=Prelude= because of the way =Relude= redefines =show= (and we need to use the =show=
that =Prelude= uses; see [[https://github.com/snoyberg/basic-prelude/issues/67#issuecomment-221628073][this related discussion for the Basic Prelude library, a
Relude alternative, for reference]]).

#+name: instance:Show Stylemask
#+begin_src haskell
instance Show StyleMask where
  show (StyleMask a) = "styleMaskFrom " <> x
    where
      x =
        Prelude.show
          . catMaybes
          . map
            ( \(i, b) ->
                if b
                  then (Just $ (toEnum i :: Style))
                  else Nothing
            )
          . zip [0 ..]
          $ toListLE a
#+end_src

The shown text string is prefixed with [[f:styleMaskFrom]] because that function is
used as a constructor function to create a =StyleMask= easily from scratch;
otherwise we'd have to write magic-looking numbers to construct =StyleMask=
values, such as in test cases like [[ti:0005-styled-strings-messy-boundaries]]. This
follows the tradition used in =Data.Map= where they also use a constructor
function (=fromList=) when =show=-ing a =Map= data structure.

#+name: f:styleMaskFrom
#+begin_src haskell
styleMaskFrom :: [Style] -> StyleMask
styleMaskFrom ss = StyleMask $ foldl' f (0 :: Word8) ss
  where
    f :: Word8 -> Style -> Word8
    f acc s = setBit acc (fromEnum s)
#+end_src

Similarly, we sometimes want to go the other direction --- that is, deconstruct
a =StyleMask= into =[Style]=. So we have [[f:maskToStyles]].

#+name: f:maskToStyles
#+begin_src haskell
maskToStyles :: StyleMask -> [Style]
maskToStyles (StyleMask mask) = foldl' f [] $ zip [0 ..] $ toListLE mask
  where
    f :: [Style] -> (Int, Bool) -> [Style]
    f acc (idx, b) =
      if b
        then (toEnum idx) : acc
        else acc
#+end_src

We have a helper function [[f:toStyleMask]] to convert =Char= into a =StyleMask=. This
is used to [[f:styleMarkerParser][construct a style mask from a style marker]] in the input Org doc. The
=toStyleMask= function runs inside a =MonadFail= monad, because the =Char= type could
hold a value that is not a valid style marker. The use of =MonadFail= is seamless
inside =ParsecT= (the monad we use inside =OrgParser=), because =ParsecT= has a
=MonadFail= instance.

#+name: f:toStyleMask
#+begin_src haskell
toStyleMask :: (MonadFail m) => Char -> m StyleMask
toStyleMask cs = pure =<< styleToStyleMask =<< charToStyle cs

charToStyle :: (MonadFail m) => Char -> m Style
charToStyle c = case lookup c styleMarkers of
  Just style -> pure style
  Nothing -> fail $ "programmer error: unrecognized style marker " <> (show c)

styleToStyleMask :: (MonadFail m) => Style -> m StyleMask
styleToStyleMask s = pure $ StyleMask (setBit 0 $ fromEnum s)
#+end_src

[[f:styleMaskFlip]] is a helper to perform a binary XOR operation on two =StyleMask=
objects, so that callers don't have to perform the deconstruction and
reconstruction of the underlying =Word8= primitive themselves. This operation is
used to either turn on bits, or turn off bits if they're already on.

#+name: f:styleMaskFlip
#+begin_src haskell
styleMaskFlip :: StyleMask -> StyleMask -> StyleMask
styleMaskFlip (StyleMask a) (StyleMask b) = StyleMask $ a .^. b
#+end_src

We also have some helpers to check whether particular styles are active.

#+name: f:isVerbatim
#+begin_src haskell
isVerbatim :: StyleMask -> Bool
isVerbatim (StyleMask a) = testBit a (fromEnum Verbatim)

isCode :: StyleMask -> Bool
isCode (StyleMask a) = testBit a (fromEnum Code)
#+end_src

[[f:isEmptyMask]] checks whether any styles are active.

#+name: f:isEmptyMask
#+begin_src haskell
isEmptyMask :: StyleMask -> Bool
isEmptyMask (StyleMask a) = a == 0
#+end_src

We stated earlier around [[t:0005-styled-strings-leftover-active-styles]] that we don't
have to wait to see the ending style marker to start parsing styled text. While
this is true, the downside is that by the time we construct a =StyledText=, we
have no knowledge of future =StyledText= objects that have not yet been parsed.
This can lead to a proliferation of =StyledText= objects that all share the same
style, but are still parsed into separate =PToken= objects. That is, instead of
just one =PToken= with a single =StyledText= object with a sizable ~body~ field, we
could end up with ~[PToken]~ where each of the elements has a small ~body~, but with
the same overall meaning.

Obviously, having a list of =PToken= objects when we can just have a single one is
problematic, if not wasteful. We want to join up sequences of styled text that
share the same style, and make them use a single =StyledText= object. For this
purpose, we have =compressProse=.

#+name: f:compressProse
#+begin_src haskell
compressProse :: Prose -> Prose
compressProse prose =
  Prose
    . reverse
    . dropUnnecessary
    $ foldl' f [] prose.unProse
  where
    f :: [PToken] -> PToken -> [PToken]
    f [] token = token : []
    f
      ((t1@(PTokenStyledText (StyledText body1 style1))) : rest)
      t2@(PTokenStyledText (StyledText body2 style2))
        | style1 == style2 =
            (PTokenStyledText $ StyledText (T.concat [body1, body2]) style1) : rest
        | otherwise = t2 : t1 : rest
    f acc token = token : acc
    dropUnnecessary [] = []
    dropUnnecessary (((PTokenStyledText (StyledText " " _))) : rest) = rest
    dropUnnecessary (((PTokenStyledText (StyledText "" _))) : rest) = rest
    dropUnnecessary tokens = tokens
#+end_src

This function works by looking at two consecutive ~PTokenStyledText~ elements at a
time (one that's already in the accumulator (~acc~) from the previous iteration
and one from the current ~token~ at hand), joining them if they share the same
style.

=compressProse= also eliminates trailing whitespace, with =dropUnnecessary=.
Trailing whitespace can occur if the last word of a paragraph at the end of the
file ends with an active =StyleMask= followed by a newline (trailing whitespace
between paragraphs can't occur because the =lexeme= in [[f:cellParser]] will skip past
them). For example, if the input text looks like

#+begin_src org
_foo
bar_
#+end_src

then the last underscore can be followed by a newline (assuming the editor used
to edit this file is UNIX-friendly and appends a trailing newline), in which
case that newline would get parsed by the =spaceConsumer= in [[f:paragraphParser]] as
its own separate =StyledText= object as a single space character. We want to
eliminate these objects because they don't add any useful information for Lilac
itself, and so we use =dropUnnecessary= above to filter them out.

=dropUnnecessary= also removes tokens with an empty string as the =StyledText=
object. This can occur when we parse trailing newlines at the end of the line
even without style markers, from [[f:whitespaceCompressor]].

Here we test how we minimize the number of =PToken= objects; although the
individual letters are spaced out with multiple spaces, we only use two =PToken=
objects (one for the plain text and another for the bold text) and each of these
objects have just single spaces between the letters.

#+name: ti:0005-styled-strings-compression
#+header: :tangle test/Lilac/ParseSpec/0005-styled-strings-compression
#+begin_src org
a b  c   d    e      *f g  h   i    j*
#+end_src

#+name: t:0005-styled-strings-compression
#+begin_src haskell
shouldParseWith
  "0005-styled-strings-compression"
  $ nilOrgDoc
    [ CParagraph
        $ Prose
          <<Expected prose for 0005-styled-strings-compression>>
    ]
    []
#+end_src

#+name: Expected prose for 0005-styled-strings-compression
#+begin_src haskell
[ ptext "a b c d e " [],
  ptext "f g h i j" [Bold]
]
#+end_src

[[f:compressStyledText]] is like [[f:compressProse]], but only for [[data:StyledText]]. It's
used in [[id:7874b9c5-ceac-4820-8804-7732e29f5c60::f:cslJsonTextToProse][f:cslJsonTextToProse]] to cut down on the verbosity of ~StyledText~ items
generated by the conversion of ~CslJson~ to ~Prose~.

#+name: f:compressStyledText
#+begin_src haskell
compressStyledText :: [StyledText] -> [StyledText]
compressStyledText = reverse . dropUnnecessary . foldl' f []
  where
    f :: [StyledText] -> StyledText -> [StyledText]
    f [] styledText = styledText : []
    f
      ((t1@(StyledText body1 style1)) : rest)
      t2@(StyledText body2 style2)
        | style1 == style2 =
            (StyledText (T.concat [body1, body2]) style1) : rest
        | otherwise = t2 : t1 : rest
    dropUnnecessary [] = []
    dropUnnecessary ((StyledText " " _) : rest) = rest
    dropUnnecessary ((StyledText "" _) : rest) = rest
    dropUnnecessary styledTexts = styledTexts
#+end_src

** Additional tests

The simple case with no style markers.

#+name: ti:0005-styled-strings-no-style
#+header: :tangle test/Lilac/ParseSpec/0005-styled-strings-no-style
#+begin_src org
foo
#+end_src

#+name: t:0005-styled-strings-no-style
#+begin_src haskell
shouldParseWith
  "0005-styled-strings-no-style"
  $ nilOrgDoc
    [ plainParagraph "foo"
    ]
    []
#+end_src

The next case tests a simple case where one word is in bold.

#+name: ti:0005-styled-strings-bold
#+header: :tangle test/Lilac/ParseSpec/0005-styled-strings-bold
#+begin_src org
foo *bar* baz
#+end_src

#+name: t:0005-styled-strings-bold
#+begin_src haskell
shouldParseWith
  "0005-styled-strings-bold"
  $ nilOrgDoc
    [ CParagraph
        $ Prose
          [ ptext "foo " [],
            ptext "bar" [Bold],
            ptext " baz" []
          ]
    ]
    []
#+end_src

Below we mix things up a bit more, using more styles.

#+name: ti:0005-styled-strings-complex
#+header: :tangle test/Lilac/ParseSpec/0005-styled-strings-complex
#+begin_src org
,*foo /bar _underlined  bold  and  italic_/ baz* =verbatim  text= ~x  y  z~ *bold*
#+end_src

#+name: t:0005-styled-strings-complex
#+begin_src haskell
shouldParseWith
  "0005-styled-strings-complex"
  $ nilOrgDoc
    [ CParagraph
        $ Prose
          <<Expected prose for 0005-styled-strings-complex>>
    ]
    []
#+end_src

#+name: Expected prose for 0005-styled-strings-complex
#+begin_src haskell
[ ptext "foo " [Bold],
  ptext "bar " [Bold, Italic],
  ptext "underlined bold and italic" [Bold, Italic, Underline],
  ptext " baz" [Bold],
  ptext " " [],
  ptext "verbatim  text" [Verbatim],
  ptext " " [],
  ptext "x  y  z" [Code],
  ptext " " [],
  ptext "bold" [Bold]
]
#+end_src

Here's another test, which checks some edge cases when dealing with punctuation.

#+name: ti:0005-styled-strings-complex-punctuation
#+header: :tangle test/Lilac/ParseSpec/0005-styled-strings-complex-punctuation
#+begin_src org
(foo =bar=). What's up? and *that!* /(italic)/. It's #1!
#+end_src

#+name: t:0005-styled-strings-complex-punctuation
#+begin_src haskell
shouldParseWith
  "0005-styled-strings-complex-punctuation"
  $ nilOrgDoc
    [ CParagraph
        $ Prose
          <<Expected prose for 0005-styled-strings-complex-punctuation>>
    ]
    []
#+end_src

Note how we preserve the information of having no space character between the
=bar= and the closing parenthesis ")".

#+name: Expected prose for 0005-styled-strings-complex-punctuation
#+begin_src haskell
[ ptext "(foo " [],
  ptext "bar" [Verbatim],
  ptext "). What's up? and " [],
  ptext "that!" [Bold],
  ptext " " [],
  ptext "(italic)" [Italic],
  ptext ". It's #1!" []
]
#+end_src

Check that we can parse slashes inside words as slashes, not as italic style
markers.

#+name: ti:0005-styled-strings-slash-separated-words
#+header: :tangle test/Lilac/ParseSpec/0005-styled-strings-slash-separated-words
#+begin_src org
/italic fraction 1/2/ foo and/or bar /A+/B-/ +1/+2. key/value
#+end_src

#+name: t:0005-styled-strings-slash-separated-words
#+begin_src haskell
shouldParseWith
  "0005-styled-strings-slash-separated-words"
  $ nilOrgDoc
    [ CParagraph
        $ Prose
          <<Expected prose for 0005-styled-strings-slash-separated-words>>
    ]
    []
#+end_src

#+name: Expected prose for 0005-styled-strings-slash-separated-words
#+begin_src haskell
[ ptext "italic fraction 1/2" [Italic],
  ptext " foo and/or bar " [],
  ptext "A+/B-" [Italic],
  ptext " +1/+2. key/value" []
]
#+end_src

This is another test, involving the =Verbatim= style. It checks the case where we
have a style marker inside a word (not at the usual beginning or end of a word).

#+name: ti:0005-styled-strings-verbatim
#+header: :tangle test/Lilac/ParseSpec/0005-styled-strings-verbatim
#+begin_src org
(=import ./.=) hello
#+end_src

#+name: t:0005-styled-strings-verbatim
#+begin_src haskell
shouldParseWith
  "0005-styled-strings-verbatim"
  $ nilOrgDoc
    [ CParagraph
        $ Prose
          <<Expected prose for 0005-styled-strings-verbatim>>
    ]
    []
#+end_src

#+name: Expected prose for 0005-styled-strings-verbatim
#+begin_src haskell
[ ptext "(" [],
  ptext "import ./." [Verbatim],
  ptext ") hello" []
]
#+end_src

This is another test to check that the bold style marker (asterisk) is ignored
if it immediately follows a verbatim or code marker.

#+name: ti:0005-styled-strings-verbatim-code-ignore-immediate-bold
#+header: :tangle test/Lilac/ParseSpec/0005-styled-strings-verbatim-code-ignore-immediate-bold
#+begin_src org
=*a= =b*= ~*c~ ~d*~
#+end_src

#+name: t:0005-styled-strings-verbatim-code-ignore-immediate-bold
#+begin_src haskell
shouldParseWith
  "0005-styled-strings-verbatim-code-ignore-immediate-bold"
  $ nilOrgDoc
    [ CParagraph
        $ Prose
          <<Expected prose for 0005-styled-strings-verbatim-code-ignore-immediate-bold>>
    ]
    []
#+end_src

#+name: Expected prose for 0005-styled-strings-verbatim-code-ignore-immediate-bold
#+begin_src haskell
[ ptext "*a" [Verbatim],
  ptext " " [],
  ptext "b*" [Verbatim],
  ptext " " [],
  ptext "*c" [Code],
  ptext " " [],
  ptext "d*" [Code]
]
#+end_src

Verbatim and code styles take precedence over [[id:f8254d58-7a3d-4595-a265-e0d6255e6ab5][links]]. So even if you have text
that looks like a link, they will not be parsed as such if surrounded by
verbatim or code styles. The same applies for [[id:216e94b7-e037-4f3e-856a-2040d0d0a75a][inline math]], whose parser should
not apply if we're inside a verbatim (or code) region.

#+name: ti:0005-styled-strings-verbatim-precedence
#+header: :tangle test/Lilac/ParseSpec/0005-styled-strings-verbatim-precedence
#+begin_src org
=[[foo=

[[hello]] there

=\(1 + 1 = ~\(2 * 2\)~

~[[bar]]~
#+end_src

#+name: t:0005-styled-strings-verbatim-precedence
#+begin_src haskell
shouldParseWith
  "0005-styled-strings-verbatim-precedence"
  $ nilOrgDoc
    <<Expected prose for 0005-styled-strings-verbatim-precedence>>
    [PseudoEdge (1, LinkOid {oid = fromWords 0 0, name = "hello"})]
#+end_src

#+name: Expected prose for 0005-styled-strings-verbatim-precedence
#+begin_src haskell
[ CParagraph
    $ Prose
      [ ptext "[[foo" [Verbatim]
      ],
  CParagraph
    $ Prose
      [ PTokenLink
          Link
            { target =
                LinkTargetOid
                  LinkOid
                    { oid = nilOid,
                      name = "hello"
                    },
              name =
                [ stext "hello" []
                ]
            },
        ptext " there" []
      ],
  CParagraph
    $ Prose
      [ ptext "\\(1 + 1 " [Verbatim],
        ptext " " [],
        ptext "\\(2 * 2\\)" [Code]
      ],
  CParagraph
    $ Prose
      [ ptext "[[bar]]" [Code]
      ]
]
#+end_src

* Links
:PROPERTIES:
:ID:       f8254d58-7a3d-4595-a265-e0d6255e6ab5
:END:

A link has

  - a name :: what's displayed to the reader, and

  - a target :: where we want to take the reader when the click on the link
    (including any metadata).

#+name: data:Link
#+begin_src haskell
type Link :: Type
data Link = Link
  { name :: [StyledText],
    target :: LinkTarget
  }
  deriving stock (Eq, Generic, Ord, Show)
  deriving anyclass (FromJSON, ToJSON)
#+end_src

The [[data:LinkTarget]] is a rich definition of the kind of target we are linking
to. For now, this data structure only supports the following cases:

  1) [[data:LinkOid]] (links to either another part of the current Org document or
     another (external) Org document).

  2) A link to a [[id:4127feee-755d-430e-8fef-192e95143790][footnote definition]]. These also use [[data:LinkOid]] internally,
     and always refer to footnote definitions within the same Org document.

  3) URI (a typical web link, most common example being =https://...=).

  4) File path. These links are only useful because they let you go open that
     file on disk locally in Emacs. On weaving these links are checked if they
     refer to a path inside the current Git repo. If they are inside, then we
     calculate what the link /should/ target (based on weaving configuration),
     assuming that the entire Git repo is also being served somewhere on the
     internet.

     If the file path is pointing to a place outside the Git repo, then on
     weaving we display it as a path description (and not as a link), because
     the link will be broken anyway (again, assuming that only the content
     inside the Git repo is accessible by a link).

#+name: data:LinkTarget
#+begin_src haskell
type LinkTarget :: Type
data LinkTarget
  = LinkTargetOid LinkOid
  | LinkTargetLineLabel Text
  | LinkTargetFootnote Text
  | LinkTargetURI N.URI
  | LinkTargetFilePath FilePath
  deriving stock (Eq, Generic, Ord, Show)
  deriving anyclass (FromJSON, ToJSON)
#+end_src

A Noweb link points to a code block with the given =name=, as well as the [[id:96f8cbda-abca-48bd-bb89-512aaac61ca8][Org ID
of the file]] the code block comes from. The OID acts as a /namespace/, so that each
Org file is free to choose any name it wants for code blocks without fear of
other Org files that might have a code block with the same name.

We can use [[data:LinkOid]] to encode Noweb links when these links appear inside a
code block. See [[f:oidReferenceParser]] for to see the expected format of these
links.

#+name: data:LinkOid
#+begin_src haskell
type LinkOid :: Type
data LinkOid = LinkOid
  { oid :: OID,
    name :: Text
  }
  deriving stock (Eq, Generic, Ord)
#+end_src

We have a custom =Show= instance so that it's much more concise (especially when
we're marshaling and unmarshaling from JSON).

#+name: instance:Show LinkOid
#+begin_src haskell
instance Show LinkOid where
  show (LinkOid (OID uuid) name)
    | T.null name = "id:" <> show uuid
    | otherwise = "id:" <> show uuid <> "::" <> T.unpack name
#+end_src

In summary we support the following link types in an Org document:

  - ~LinkTargetOid~ :: a link to another cell or Org document, also used for Noweb
    reference inside another code block (because code blocks are just cells).
    They can be used to also link to figures, which are also cells. [[fig:PNG image][Example]].

    When inside code blocks, these use angle brackets (e.g., ~<<foo>>~). This can
    reference code blocks in other files by OID, where the syntax is
    ~<<id:...::foo>>~ where ~...~ is the OID and ~foo~ is the name of the code block
    at that OID (either using the top-level OID of the document or the OID of
    the closest enclosing heading). We have to use an OID-to-file map data
    structure (which is parsed [[id:96f8cbda-abca-48bd-bb89-512aaac61ca8][here]]).

    When used outside of code blocks, square brackets are used (e.g., ~[[target]]~
    or ~[[target][name]]~) where ~target~ is just a plain text string (such as a URL
    or some other entity in the document), and ~name~ is what is displayed to the
    user when the link is rendered.

  - ~LinkTargetLineLabel~ :: a link that points to a reference /inside/ a code block. This
    is useful for referring to different areas inside a code block, without
    having to break up the code block into multiple separate parts.

  - ~LinkTargetURI~ :: a link that points to a traditional URI; these are the most
    common types of links on the internet, the most famous example being
    anything that looks like =https://...= or =http://...=.

  - LinkTargetFilePath :: a link to a file. Although this is arbitrary when
    using Emacs, it has a limitation on weaving --- only those files that are
    also being served by the web server that is serving that page will be linked
    (otherwise the link will be broken).

There are also [[id:8fe81206-f50d-4200-a95e-99278e0ad410][Raw links]] which is prose text that looks like a URL. For example,
https://www.haskell.org is an example of a raw link. This is a convenience
facility because ~[[https://www.haskell.org]]~ behaves the same way as
~https://www.haskell.org~.

** OIDs and Emacs
:PROPERTIES:
:ID:       96f8cbda-abca-48bd-bb89-512aaac61ca8
:END:

Lilac relies on Org files that are written in Emacs (Emacs is the best
environment for writing Org files). When the ~org-id-locations-file~ and
~org-id-track-globally~ variables are set, Emacs remembers which files have which
Org IDs (where the "ID" is the OID generated by ~org-id-get-create~ and
~org-id-store-link~). Emacs then saves this information to the value of
~org-id-locations-file~.

Historically Lilac used to parse these files generated by Emacs, but it has
moved on to a compilation pipeline that doesn't need to rely on these files
coming from Emacs. If Lilac wants to bring back tangling and weaving based
purely on Org files without the compilation process, it will be forced to read
these OID location files again. Below is a brief discussion of the format of
these files to serve as a historical reference.

The syntax is of a list of list of strings, in Emacs lisp. The inner lists have
the relative file path of the Org file as the first element, followed by the
OIDs. Each Org ID is just a UUIDv4 string. The OIDs are in reverse order
(probably because Emacs grows the list right-to-left, just like Haskell's ~:~ (aka
"cons") function).

Below is an example, with newlines inserted for better presentation (Emacs does
not use newlines here, and so in the wild everything is in one line):

#+begin_src emacs-lisp
(("note/raw/recreation.org" "a1e6d0d5-4540-423b-864e-b96ad06c56eb")
 ("../prog/lilac/lilac.org" "3c576a02-418b-431b-ab96-e4936e977fb7"
                            "ce9af5ef-6559-4ffb-82c1-a439214eda6d"
                            "8f5752c0-6162-4de6-9ceb-f78ba584722c"
                            "b449a097-d702-4fd6-98de-cb81f7e49177"))
#+end_src

The information stored in this file is global, because Emacs will update it
whenever it encounters Org files.

** Standalone link parser

The standalone link parser [[f:linkParser]] can parse links that can be embedded
inside prose text.

#+name: f:linkParser
#+begin_src haskell
linkParser :: OrgParser Link
linkParser = do
  failIfVerbatimOrCode

  MP.choice
    [ MP.try lineLabelLinkParser,
      MP.try oidLinkParser,
      MP.try protocolWebLinkParser,
      MP.try fileLinkParser,
      MP.try footnoteLinkParser,
      MP.try rawLinkParser
    ]
#+end_src

The call to [[f:failIfVerbatimOrCode]] fails the parser if either the [[f:isVerbatim][verbatim or
code style is active]]. This is because the verbatim and code styles take
precedence over parsing links. See, for example
[[t:0005-styled-strings-verbatim-precedence]] of the sort of behavior we expect.

#+name: f:failIfVerbatimOrCode
#+begin_src haskell
failIfVerbatimOrCode :: OrgParser ()
failIfVerbatimOrCode = do
  orgParserState <- get
  let mask = orgParserState.styleMask
  when (isVerbatim mask || isCode mask)
    $ fail "aborting because verbatim or code style is active"
#+end_src

By conditionally failing [[f:linkParser]] with [[f:failIfVerbatimOrCode]], we also allow
other parsers (such as [[f:styledTextParser]]) to succeed (see [[f:proseParser]] for the
interaction of this parser with these other parsers).

Note that in [[f:linkParser]] we don't parse [[id:81e1c841-5de8-4111-9b18-f33bea904122][Noweb links]] here because they can only
exist inside [[id:cae993e5-02bd-4f89-a6d3-20b41adaff8f][code blocks]].

** OID links
:PROPERTIES:
:ID:       dabccd5e-930e-4571-899f-1bfbdeab3d5e
:END:

The [[f:oidLinkParser]] closely resembles the [[f:nowebLinkTargetParser]], because they
both encode a [[data:LinkOid]], the only difference being where they are used (Noweb
links are used exclusively inside code blocks to refer to other code blocks).

#+name: f:oidLinkParser
#+begin_src haskell
oidLinkParser :: OrgParser Link
oidLinkParser = do
  _ <- string "[["
  avoidParsingKnownProtocols
  (oid, name) <- oidReferenceParser "[]" "]]"
  orgParserState <- get
  let mask = orgParserState.styleMask
      implicitName
        | T.null name = "id:" <> T.show oid
        | otherwise = name
      implicitNameStyledText =
        StyledText
          { body = implicitName,
            style = mask
          }

  _ <- string "]"
  displayName <-
    MP.choice
      [ MP.try $ string "]" >> pure [implicitNameStyledText],
        MP.try linkDisplayNameParser
      ]
  let linkOid =
        LinkOid
          { oid = oid,
            name = name
          }
      edge = PseudoEdge (orgParserState.cellNumber, linkOid)
  modify (\s -> s {OrgParserState.pseudoEdges = edge : s.pseudoEdges})
  pure
    $ Link
      { target = LinkTargetOid linkOid,
        name = displayName
      }
  where
    avoidParsingKnownProtocols :: OrgParser ()
    avoidParsingKnownProtocols =
      MP.notFollowedBy
        . MP.choice
        . map (MP.try . string)
        $ protocolsWeb
        <> protocolsEmacs
#+end_src

[[f:oidLinkParser]] creates a "pseudo" edge, from the current ~cellNumber~ to the
~linkOid~. This is used for [[id:b1057136-48d5-402e-8e55-5a7e6a08efe4][backlink]] generation.

[[f:linkDisplayNameParser]] just gets the name of the text to display. It is reused
in [[f:protocolWebLinkParser]]. It uses the ~proseParser'~ helper which is very
similar to the [[f:proseParser]], except that instead of returning [[data:PToken]]
values, it returns the finer-grained [[data:StyledText]]. We want to be careful
about how we treat whitespace (especially across indentation levels), so we
reuse the same [[f:proseSpaceConsumer]] that [[f:paragraphParser]] uses.

#+name: f:linkDisplayNameParser
#+begin_src haskell
linkDisplayNameParser :: OrgParser [StyledText]
linkDisplayNameParser = do
  orgParserState <- get
  modify (\s -> s {insideLinkDisplayName = True})
  linkDisplayName <-
    MP.between
      (string "[")
      (string "]]")
      (proseParser' $ proseSpaceConsumer orgParserState.continuationIndent)
  modify (\s -> s {insideLinkDisplayName = False})
  pure linkDisplayName
  where
    proseParser' :: OrgParser Text -> OrgParser [StyledText]
    proseParser' spaceConsumer = do
      a <- MP.choice baseParsers
      b <- MP.many $ MP.choice (spaceParser : baseParsers)
      pure . filter nonEmpty $ a : b
      where
        spaceParser = do
          s <- whitespaceCompressor spaceConsumer
          pure StyledText {body = s, style = styleMaskFrom []}
        baseParsers =
          [ styleMarkerParser,
            styledTextParser
          ]
        nonEmpty :: StyledText -> Bool
        nonEmpty styledText = not $ T.null styledText.body
#+end_src

#+name: ti:0006-links-oids
#+header: :tangle test/Lilac/ParseSpec/0006-links-oids
#+begin_src org
[[id:442c807f-c9d9-45a7-88bf-ffffffffffff]]
[[id:442c807f-c9d9-45a7-88bf-ffffffffffff][bar]]
[[id:442c807f-c9d9-45a7-88bf-ffffffffffff][bar =[]=]]
[[id:442c807f-c9d9-45a7-88bf-ffffffffffff][bar
ok]]
[[id:442c807f-c9d9-45a7-88bf-ffffffffffff::foo][abc]]
[[foo][abc]]

  - link name across list indentation [[foo][/italic/
    def ghi]
    !]]

  - link target with spaces [[foo bar
    baz]]

  - oid with spaces [[id:442c807f-c9d9-45a7-88bf-ffffffffffff::a b
    c]]
#+end_src

#+name: t:0006-links-oids
#+begin_src haskell
shouldParseWith
  "0006-links-oids"
  ( nilOrgDoc
      [ CParagraph
          $ Prose
            [ PTokenLink
                Link
                  { target =
                      LinkTargetOid
                        LinkOid
                          { oid = fromWords 0x442c807fc9d945a7 0x88bfffffffffffff,
                            name = ""
                          },
                    name =
                      [ stext "id:442c807f-c9d9-45a7-88bf-ffffffffffff" []
                      ]
                  },
              ptext " " [],
              PTokenLink
                Link
                  { target =
                      LinkTargetOid
                        LinkOid
                          { oid = fromWords 0x442c807fc9d945a7 0x88bfffffffffffff,
                            name = ""
                          },
                    name =
                      [ stext "bar" []
                      ]
                  },
              ptext " " [],
              PTokenLink
                Link
                  { target =
                      LinkTargetOid
                        LinkOid
                          { oid = fromWords 0x442c807fc9d945a7 0x88bfffffffffffff,
                            name = ""
                          },
                    name =
                      [ stext "bar" [],
                        stext " " [],
                        stext "[]" [Verbatim]
                      ]
                  },
              ptext " " [],
              PTokenLink
                Link
                  { target =
                      LinkTargetOid
                        LinkOid
                          { oid = fromWords 0x442c807fc9d945a7 0x88bfffffffffffff,
                            name = ""
                          },
                    name =
                      [ stext "bar" [],
                        stext " " [],
                        stext "ok" []
                      ]
                  },
              ptext " " [],
              PTokenLink
                Link
                  { target =
                      LinkTargetOid
                        LinkOid
                          { oid = fromWords 0x442c807fc9d945a7 0x88bfffffffffffff,
                            name = "foo"
                          },
                    name = [stext "abc" []]
                  },
              ptext " " [],
              PTokenLink
                Link
                  { target =
                      LinkTargetOid
                        LinkOid
                          { oid = nilOid,
                            name = "foo"
                          },
                    name = [stext "abc" []]
                  }
            ],
        CListItem
          [ LUnorderedStart,
            LItemStart
          ],
        CParagraph
          $ Prose
            [ ptext "link name across list indentation " [],
              PTokenLink
                Link
                  { name =
                      [ stext "italic" [Italic],
                        stext " " [],
                        stext "def" [],
                        stext " " [],
                        stext "ghi]" [],
                        stext " " [],
                        stext "!" []
                      ],
                    target =
                      LinkTargetOid
                        LinkOid
                          { oid = nilOid,
                            name = "foo"
                          }
                  }
            ],
        CListItem
          [ LItemEnd,
            LItemStart
          ],
        CParagraph
          $ Prose
            [ ptext "link target with spaces " [],
              PTokenLink
                Link
                  { name = [stext "foo bar baz" []],
                    target =
                      LinkTargetOid
                        LinkOid
                          { oid = nilOid,
                            name = "foo bar baz"
                          }
                  }
            ],
        CListItem
          [ LItemEnd,
            LItemStart
          ],
        CParagraph
          $ Prose
            [ ptext "oid with spaces " [],
              PTokenLink
                Link
                  { name = [stext "a b c" []],
                    target =
                      LinkTargetOid
                        LinkOid
                          { oid = fromWords 0x442c807fc9d945a7 0x88bfffffffffffff,
                            name = "a b c"
                          }
                  }
            ],
        CListItem
          [ LItemEnd,
            LUnorderedEnd
          ]
      ]
      [ PseudoEdge (6, LinkOid {oid = fromWords 0x442c807fc9d945a7 0x88bfffffffffffff, name = "a b c"}),
        PseudoEdge (4, LinkOid {oid = fromWords 0x0000000000000000 0x0000000000000000, name = "foo bar baz"}),
        PseudoEdge (2, LinkOid {oid = fromWords 0x0000000000000000 0x0000000000000000, name = "foo"}),
        PseudoEdge (0, LinkOid {oid = fromWords 0x0000000000000000 0x0000000000000000, name = "foo"}),
        PseudoEdge (0, LinkOid {oid = fromWords 0x442c807fc9d945a7 0x88bfffffffffffff, name = "foo"}),
        PseudoEdge (0, LinkOid {oid = fromWords 0x442c807fc9d945a7 0x88bfffffffffffff, name = ""}),
        PseudoEdge (0, LinkOid {oid = fromWords 0x442c807fc9d945a7 0x88bfffffffffffff, name = ""}),
        PseudoEdge (0, LinkOid {oid = fromWords 0x442c807fc9d945a7 0x88bfffffffffffff, name = ""}),
        PseudoEdge (0, LinkOid {oid = fromWords 0x442c807fc9d945a7 0x88bfffffffffffff, name = ""})
      ]
  )
#+end_src

If we encounter a link in the middle of a paragraph (where the ~OID~ portion is
broken up incorrectly such that there is additional white space at the beginning
of a line), we should still be able to parse the ~\n ~ as a single space
character because that's almost always the intent. This situation can happen
when invoking =fill-paragraph= in Emacs with =org-toggle-link-display=.

So in [[ti:0006-links-oids-lenient-whitespace]], even though the string (if we parse
it naively) should look like ~with\n newline~, that ~\n ~ will get compressed so
that we just get ~with newline~ instead.

#+name: ti:0006-links-oids-lenient-whitespace
#+header: :tangle test/Lilac/ParseSpec/0006-links-oids-lenient-whitespace
#+begin_src org
Hello [[id:442c807f-c9d9-45a7-88bf-ffffffffffff::target name with
 newline and space is still treated as space][foo]]
#+end_src

#+name: t:0006-links-oids-lenient-whitespace
#+begin_src haskell
shouldParseWith
  "0006-links-oids-lenient-whitespace"
  ( nilOrgDoc
      [ CParagraph
          $ Prose
            [ ptext "Hello " [],
              PTokenLink
                Link
                  { target =
                      LinkTargetOid
                        LinkOid
                          { oid = fromWords 0x442c807fc9d945a7 0x88bfffffffffffff,
                            name = "target name with newline and space is still treated as space"
                          },
                    name = [stext "foo" []]
                  }
            ]
      ]
      [PseudoEdge (0, LinkOid {oid = fromWords 0x442c807fc9d945a7 0x88bfffffffffffff, name = "target name with newline and space is still treated as space"})]
  )
#+end_src

** Line labels
:PROPERTIES:
:ID:       da4aa2a7-3896-4969-aa5e-5e3d842c35db
:END:

Line labels are embedded inside a block, and can be linked to from outside the
block. Below is an example; we give two code blocks, the first with the line
labels disabled and the second with the same content but with line labels
enabled, so that you can see the full syntax if you're reading this from HTML:

#+header: :line-label off
#+begin_src emacs-lisp
(save-excursion              ; (ref:a)
   (goto-char (point-min))   ; (ref:foo)
#+end_src

#+begin_src emacs-lisp
(save-excursion              ; (ref:a)
   (goto-char (point-min))   ; (ref:foo)
#+end_src

The ~(ref:a)~ and ~(ref:foo)~ will become targets that can be linked to via ~[[(a)]]~
and ~[[(foo)]]~. So now you can write [[(a)]] and [[(foo)]] to refer to specific lines in
the example above.

The line label must start with a ~(ref:~ and end with a closing parenthesis ~)~, as
[[f:lineLabelParser]] demonstrates:

#+name: f:lineLabelParser
#+begin_src haskell
lineLabelParser :: OrgParser LinkTarget
lineLabelParser = do
  _ <- string "(ref:"
  o <- MP.getOffset
  lineLabel <- lineLabelRefParser
  _ <- string ")"
  assertUniqueLineLabel lineLabel o
  pure $ LinkTargetLineLabel lineLabel
#+end_src

Valid characters for a line label are handled by [[f:lineLabelRefParser]]. This uses
[[f:compressedTextParser]] to let the user refer to line labels more freely, without
having to be exact about whitespace; see the discussion at
[[f:compressedTextParser]] for more details.

#+name: f:lineLabelRefParser
#+begin_src haskell
lineLabelRefParser :: OrgParser Text
lineLabelRefParser =
  MP.label "line label" $ compressedTextParser "()[]<>" ">>"
#+end_src

The line labels must be unique inside a document; otherwise you'll run into a
parse error, per [[f:assertUniqueLineLabel]]. [[f:assertUniqueLineLabel]] is virtually
the same as [[f:assertUniqueCellName]].

#+name: f:assertUniqueLineLabel
#+begin_src haskell
assertUniqueLineLabel :: Text -> Int -> OrgParser ()
assertUniqueLineLabel lineLabel offset = do
  orgParserState <- get
  when (member lineLabel orgParserState.lineLabels) $ do
    MP.setOffset offset
    fail
      $ "line label "
      <> show lineLabel
      <> " is not unique"
  let num = orgParserState.lineLabelNumber + 1
  put
    orgParserState
      { lineLabels =
          insert
            lineLabel
            num
            orgParserState.lineLabels,
        lineLabelNumber = num
      }
#+end_src

#+name: ti:0006-unique-line-labels
#+header: :tangle test/Lilac/ParseSpec/0006-unique-line-labels
#+header: :line-label off
#+begin_src org
,#+begin_src sh
a (ref:x)
,#+end_src

,#+begin_src sh
b (ref:x)
,#+end_src
#+end_src

#+name: t:0006-unique-line-labels
#+begin_src haskell
shouldFailWithMessage
  "0006-unique-line-labels"
  "line label \"x\" is not unique"
  58
#+end_src

The [[f:lineLabelParser]] is used in [[f:bTokenParser]], if the block being parsed does
not have ~#+header: :line-label off~.

The syntax for linking to a line label ~(ref:foo)~ is ~[[(foo)]]~. Note that the
parentheses surround the line label, to distinguish it from other cells that can
be named (via ~#+name: ...~, such as other blocks). A cell's name may not begin
with an opening parenthesis, so that ~[[(foo)]]~ always links to the line label
named ~foo~, not a cell named ~(foo)~; this prohibition of an opening parenthesis is
codified in [[f:metaEntryParser]].

Parsing of line label links is handled by [[f:lineLabelLinkParser]].

#+name: f:lineLabelLinkParser
#+begin_src haskell
lineLabelLinkParser :: OrgParser Link
lineLabelLinkParser = do
  _ <- string "[[("
  lineLabel <- lineLabelRefParser
  _ <- string ")]]"
  modify
    ( \s ->
        s
          { OrgParserState.lineLabelReferences =
              Set.insert lineLabel s.lineLabelReferences
          }
    )
  pure
    $ Link
      { target = LinkTargetLineLabel lineLabel,
        name = []
      }
#+end_src

Unlike other links that use the double bracket syntax, these line label links
cannot be given a custom name; instead they are always named numerically when
woven (see [[id:4ae846e7-b95c-4996-99e8-8ce6a7e0fdc1::f:weaveLink][f:weaveLink]]). During weaving, the line labels are always numbered
starting at number ~1~, and increment upwards. Each block resets the counter back
to ~1~. The ~lineLabels~ and ~lineLabelNumber~ fields in [[data:OrgParserState]] keep
track of this numbering strategy. And just like for footnotes, we check how
these line labels are used in [[f:reconcileLineLabels]], which is based heavily off
of [[f:reconcileFootnotes]].

#+name: f:reconcileLineLabels
#+begin_src haskell
reconcileLineLabels :: OrgParser (Map Text Int)
reconcileLineLabels = do
  orgParserState <- get
  let referenced = orgParserState.lineLabelReferences
      defined = M.keysSet orgParserState.lineLabels
      brokenLinks = Set.difference referenced defined
      unusedDefs = Set.difference defined referenced
      hasBrokenLinks = length brokenLinks /= 0
      hasUnusedDefs = length unusedDefs /= 0
      brokenLinksMsg = T.unpack . unwords $ toList brokenLinks
      unusedDefsMsg = T.unpack . unwords $ toList unusedDefs
  if
    | hasBrokenLinks -> fail $ "broken line label links: " <> brokenLinksMsg
    | hasUnusedDefs -> fail $ "unused line labels: " <> unusedDefsMsg
    | otherwise -> pure orgParserState.lineLabels
#+end_src

** Links to recognized web protocols

Links to web protocols like ~https://~ should be parsed as a ~LinkTargetURI~, but
not just for [[id:8fe81206-f50d-4200-a95e-99278e0ad410][Raw links]] but also those links found inside square brackets, like
~[[http://abc.xyz]]~ and ~[[http://abc.xyz][foo]]~.

#+name: f:protocolWebLinkParser
#+begin_src haskell
protocolWebLinkParser :: OrgParser Link
protocolWebLinkParser = do
  maybeUri <-
    MP.between
      (string "[[")
      (string "]")
      uriParser

  orgParserState <- get
  let mask = orgParserState.styleMask
      implicitName = maybeUri
      implicitNameStyledText =
        StyledText
          { body = implicitName,
            style = mask
          }
  displayName <-
    MP.choice
      [ MP.try $ string "]" >> pure [implicitNameStyledText],
        MP.try linkDisplayNameParser
      ]
  case N.parseURI (T.unpack maybeUri) of
    Nothing -> fail $ "(protocol web link) not a valid URI: " <> (T.unpack maybeUri)
    Just uri ->
      pure
        $ Link
          { target = LinkTargetURI uri,
            name = displayName
          }
  where
    uriParser :: OrgParser Text
    uriParser = do
      protocol <- MP.choice $ map (MP.try . string) protocolsWeb
      rest <- MP.takeWhile1P (Just "name character") (/= ']')
      pure $ protocol <> rest
#+end_src

#+name: ti:0006-links-web-protocols
#+header: :tangle test/Lilac/ParseSpec/0006-links-web-protocols
#+begin_src org
[[http://foo.org]]
[[https://foo.org]]
[[https://foo.org][hello]]
#+end_src

#+name: t:0006-links-web-protocols
#+begin_src haskell
shouldParseWith
  "0006-links-web-protocols"
  ( nilOrgDoc
      [ CParagraph
          $ Prose
            [ PTokenLink
                Link
                  { target =
                      LinkTargetURI
                        N.URI
                          { N.uriScheme = "http:",
                            N.uriAuthority =
                              Just
                                N.URIAuth
                                  { N.uriUserInfo = "",
                                    N.uriRegName = "foo.org",
                                    N.uriPort = ""
                                  },
                            N.uriPath = "",
                            N.uriQuery = "",
                            N.uriFragment = ""
                          },
                    name = [stext "http://foo.org" []]
                  },
              ptext " " [],
              PTokenLink
                Link
                  { target =
                      LinkTargetURI
                        N.URI
                          { N.uriScheme = "https:",
                            N.uriAuthority =
                              Just
                                N.URIAuth
                                  { N.uriUserInfo = "",
                                    N.uriRegName = "foo.org",
                                    N.uriPort = ""
                                  },
                            N.uriPath = "",
                            N.uriQuery = "",
                            N.uriFragment = ""
                          },
                    name = [stext "https://foo.org" []]
                  },
              ptext " " [],
              PTokenLink
                Link
                  { target =
                      LinkTargetURI
                        N.URI
                          { N.uriScheme = "https:",
                            N.uriAuthority =
                              Just
                                N.URIAuth
                                  { N.uriUserInfo = "",
                                    N.uriRegName = "foo.org",
                                    N.uriPort = ""
                                  },
                            N.uriPath = "",
                            N.uriQuery = "",
                            N.uriFragment = ""
                          },
                    name = [stext "hello" []]
                  }
            ]
      ]
      []
  )
#+end_src

** Filesystem links

Orgmode lets you create links like

#+begin_src org
[[file:foo]]
#+end_src

where ~foo~ is a path on the local filesystem (you can select the path using
completion from Emacs). These links are useful for linking out to a file which
you can open up using Emacs.

Practically speaking, in only makes sense to use these links to point to files
that are already tracked by the Git repo that we're in (if we're using Lilac as
a static site generator), because otherwise we might risk creating a broken link
(assuming that the published site only includes files that are tracked in Git).

#+name: f:fileLinkParser
#+begin_src haskell
fileLinkParser :: OrgParser Link
fileLinkParser = do
  maybeFilePath <-
    MP.between
      (string "[[")
      (string "]")
      filePathParser

  orgParserState <- get
  let mask = orgParserState.styleMask
      implicitName = maybeFilePath
      implicitNameStyledText =
        StyledText
          { body = implicitName,
            style = mask
          }
      filePath = T.unpack maybeFilePath
  displayName <-
    MP.choice
      [ MP.try $ string "]" >> pure [implicitNameStyledText],
        MP.try linkDisplayNameParser
      ]
  if
    | Just osPath <- P.encodeUtf filePath,
      P.isValid osPath ->
        pure
          $ Link
            { target = LinkTargetFilePath filePath,
              name = displayName
            }
    | otherwise -> fail $ "not a valid file path: " <> filePath
  where
    filePathParser :: OrgParser Text
    filePathParser = do
      _ <- string "file:"
      fp <- MP.takeWhile1P (Just "name character") (/= ']')
      pure fp
#+end_src

Here are some basic links.

#+name: ti:0006-links
#+header: :tangle test/Lilac/ParseSpec/0006-links
#+begin_src org
foo [[link-target-only]]

,* [[link-target][link-name]]

[[a]] b
#+end_src

#+name: t:0006-links
#+begin_src haskell
shouldParseWithState
  "0006-links"
  ( nilOrgDoc
      [ CParagraph
          $ Prose
            [ ptext "foo " [],
              PTokenLink
                Link
                  { target =
                      LinkTargetOid
                        LinkOid
                          { oid = nilOid,
                            name = "link-target-only"
                          },
                    name = [stext "link-target-only" []]
                  }
            ],
        CHeading
          Heading
            { level = 1,
              section = [1],
              body =
                Prose
                  [ PTokenLink
                      Link
                        { target =
                            LinkTargetOid
                              LinkOid
                                { oid = nilOid,
                                  name = "link-target"
                                },
                          name = [stext "link-name" []]
                        }
                  ],
              meta = fromList []
            },
        CParagraph
          $ Prose
            [ PTokenLink
                Link
                  { target =
                      LinkTargetOid
                        LinkOid
                          { oid = nilOid,
                            name = "a"
                          },
                    name = [stext "a" []]
                  },
              ptext " b" []
            ]
      ]
      [ PseudoEdge (2, LinkOid {oid = fromWords 0x0000000000000000 0x0000000000000000, name = "a"}),
        PseudoEdge (0, LinkOid {oid = fromWords 0 0, name = "link-target-only"})
      ]
  )
  defaultOrgParserState
    { headingOidStack = [Nothing],
      headingLevel = 1,
      headingSectionNumber = [1]
    }
#+end_src

Bracketed links can be preceded by ~(~ which means [[f:linkParser]] won't be
able to parse it directly; however, in [[f:textParser]] (specifically ~normalChar~) we
take care to ensure to stop consuming input if we see a double left bracket ~[[~.
This way, the input will look like ~[[...~ which will then allow
[[f:linkParser]] to parse it.

#+name: ti:0006-links-inside-parentheses
#+header: :tangle test/Lilac/ParseSpec/0006-links-inside-parentheses
#+begin_src org
([[file:foo/bar]])
#+end_src

#+name: t:0006-links-inside-parentheses
#+begin_src haskell
shouldParseWith
  "0006-links-inside-parentheses"
  $ nilOrgDoc
    [ CParagraph
        $ Prose
          [ ptext "(" [],
            PTokenLink
              Link
                { target = LinkTargetFilePath "foo/bar",
                  name = [stext "foo/bar" []]
                },
            ptext ")" []
          ]
    ]
    []
#+end_src

Sometimes we have text that look like links initially but turn out to be
non-links.

#+name: ti:0006-links-nonlink
#+header: :tangle test/Lilac/ParseSpec/0006-links-nonlink
#+begin_src org
[hi]
#+end_src

#+name: t:0006-links-nonlink
#+begin_src haskell
shouldParseWith
  "0006-links-nonlink"
  $ nilOrgDoc
    [ CParagraph $ Prose [ptext "[hi]" []]
    ]
    []
#+end_src

** Raw links
:PROPERTIES:
:ID:       8fe81206-f50d-4200-a95e-99278e0ad410
:END:

The [[f:rawLinkParser]] parses text that look like URLs into ~LinkTargetURI~.

#+name: f:rawLinkParser
#+begin_src haskell
rawLinkParser :: OrgParser Link
rawLinkParser = do
  _ <- MP.try . MP.lookAhead $ do
    _ <- MP.takeWhile1P (Just "(raw link) protocol") isAlphaChar
    _ <- char ':'
    pure ()
  candidate <- MP.some $ MP.try remainderChar
  case N.parseURI candidate of
    Nothing -> fail $ "(raw link) not a valid URI: " <> candidate
    Just uri ->
      pure
        Link
          { target = LinkTargetURI uri,
            name =
              [ StyledText
                  { body = T.pack candidate,
                    style = styleMaskFrom []
                  }
              ]
          }
  where
    remainderChar :: OrgParser Char
    remainderChar = do
      c <- MP.satisfy N.isAllowedInURI
      when (isPunctuation c && c /= '/') $ do
        MP.notFollowedBy (char ' ')
        MP.notFollowedBy (char '\n')
      pure c
#+end_src

The tricky part is in the =remainderChar=. Essentially, if we see a raw link
followed by some punctuation, like https://funloop.org, then we want the
punctuation plus the whitespace immediately after it to be excluded from the
link. The punctuation may or may not be part of the allowed characters in
=N.isAllowedInURI=. We fix this problem by running some additional checks if we
ever encounter punctuation, with [[f:isPunctuation]], where the [[f:isAlphaNumChar]]
helper is taken from [[https://hackage.haskell.org/package/network-uri][network-uri]].

However, the above rule about excluding punctuation doesn't apply for the ~/~
(slash) character because sometimes a URL may end with a slash. In such cases,
we would still want to include the slash it as part of the URL.

The [[f:isPunctuation]] parser (and its parts) are generic and useful enough that we
expose them as top-level functions.

#+name: f:isPunctuation
#+begin_src haskell
isPunctuation :: Char -> Bool
isPunctuation = not . isAlphaNumChar
#+end_src

#+name: f:isAlphaNumChar
#+begin_src haskell
isAlphaNumChar :: Char -> Bool
isAlphaNumChar c = isAlphaChar c || isDigitChar c
#+end_src

#+name: f:isAlphaChar
#+begin_src haskell
isAlphaChar :: Char -> Bool
isAlphaChar c = (c >= 'A' && c <= 'Z') || (c >= 'a' && c <= 'z')
#+end_src

#+name: f:isDigitChar
#+begin_src haskell
isDigitChar :: Char -> Bool
isDigitChar c = c >= '0' && c <= '9'
#+end_src

#+name: ti:0006-links-raw
#+header: :tangle test/Lilac/ParseSpec/0006-links-raw
#+begin_src org
See https://foo.org, or https://bar.net.
Try http://localhost:3000?
Go to ftp://10.0.0.1!
https://a.com/path/to/something/
#+end_src

#+name: t:0006-links-raw
#+begin_src haskell
shouldParseWith
  "0006-links-raw"
  $ nilOrgDoc
    [ CParagraph
        $ Prose
          [ ptext "See " [],
            PTokenLink
              Link
                { target =
                    LinkTargetURI
                      N.URI
                        { N.uriScheme = "https:",
                          N.uriAuthority =
                            Just
                              N.URIAuth
                                { N.uriUserInfo = "",
                                  N.uriRegName = "foo.org",
                                  N.uriPort = ""
                                },
                          N.uriPath = "",
                          N.uriQuery = "",
                          N.uriFragment = ""
                        },
                  name = [stext "https://foo.org" []]
                },
            ptext ", or " [],
            PTokenLink
              Link
                { target =
                    LinkTargetURI
                      N.URI
                        { N.uriScheme = "https:",
                          N.uriAuthority =
                            Just
                              N.URIAuth
                                { N.uriUserInfo = "",
                                  N.uriRegName = "bar.net",
                                  N.uriPort = ""
                                },
                          N.uriPath = "",
                          N.uriQuery = "",
                          N.uriFragment = ""
                        },
                  name = [stext "https://bar.net" []]
                },
            ptext ". Try " [],
            PTokenLink
              Link
                { target =
                    LinkTargetURI
                      N.URI
                        { N.uriScheme = "http:",
                          N.uriAuthority =
                            Just
                              N.URIAuth
                                { N.uriUserInfo = "",
                                  N.uriRegName = "localhost",
                                  N.uriPort = ":3000"
                                },
                          N.uriPath = "",
                          N.uriQuery = "",
                          N.uriFragment = ""
                        },
                  name = [stext "http://localhost:3000" []]
                },
            ptext "? Go to " [],
            PTokenLink
              Link
                { target =
                    LinkTargetURI
                      N.URI
                        { N.uriScheme = "ftp:",
                          N.uriAuthority =
                            Just
                              N.URIAuth
                                { N.uriUserInfo = "",
                                  N.uriRegName = "10.0.0.1",
                                  N.uriPort = ""
                                },
                          N.uriPath = "",
                          N.uriQuery = "",
                          N.uriFragment = ""
                        },
                  name = [stext "ftp://10.0.0.1" []]
                },
            ptext "! " [],
            PTokenLink
              Link
                { target =
                    LinkTargetURI
                      N.URI
                        { N.uriScheme = "https:",
                          N.uriAuthority =
                            Just
                              N.URIAuth
                                { N.uriUserInfo = "",
                                  N.uriRegName = "a.com",
                                  N.uriPort = ""
                                },
                          N.uriPath = "/path/to/something/",
                          N.uriQuery = "",
                          N.uriFragment = ""
                        },
                  name = [stext "https://a.com/path/to/something/" []]
                }
          ]
    ]
    []
#+end_src

** Noweb links
:PROPERTIES:
:ID:       81e1c841-5de8-4111-9b18-f33bea904122
:END:

=LinkOid= links to Noweb references. We've been using Noweb references already
in this document, but here's an isolated example.

#+begin_src sh
echo <<LinkOid example>>
#+end_src

The block above refers to a Noweb reference named =LinkOid example=. We define
it below:

#+name: LinkOid example
#+begin_src sh
hello world
#+end_src

Note that if no such Noweb reference is defined, Lilac will error out. See
[[f:nowebLinkTargetParser]] for parsing details (it is discussed along with [[id:cae993e5-02bd-4f89-a6d3-20b41adaff8f][Blocks]]
because that's the only place where these links may be defined).

Noweb links are powered by [[id:f8254d58-7a3d-4595-a265-e0d6255e6ab5::data:LinkOid][data:LinkOid]] which can reference blocks in the
current Org file or any other Org file (as long as those other Org files are
[[id:c80fd6a6-7eb2-4672-99cc-a5a10c1fda69][compiled]] and [[id:75173d6f-c210-46a0-8acf-689deeacd250][archived]] together).

The ability for Noweb links to reference other files is not part of Orgmode and
is Lilac's own extension. This allows source code blocks to be defined across
multiple Org files if needed. One simple example is version information that
we might want to define only in a single place, but which might be referenced
in other places, across multiple Org files.

** Footnotes
:PROPERTIES:
:ID:       4127feee-755d-430e-8fef-192e95143790
:END:

Footnotes are essentially named paragraphs. They look like this in practice:

#+begin_src org
Hello world [fn:foo].

...

,* Footnotes

[fn:foo] A paragraph that defines this footnote.
#+end_src

So a footnote is made up of both the link to the footnote from the main text,
and a definition of the footnote at the bottom. Both the link and the definition
share the same footnote reference, which is parsed with the
[[f:footnoteReferenceParser]].

#+name: f:footnoteReferenceParser
#+begin_src haskell
footnoteReferenceParser :: OrgParser Text
footnoteReferenceParser = do
  _ <- string "[fn:"
  name <- MP.takeWhile1P (Just "footnote name character") (/= ']')
  _ <- string "]"
  pure name
#+end_src

When parsing the link to a footnote, we parse it with the
~LinkTargetFootnote~ constructor to distinguish it from ~LinkTargetOid~ which are
generic links to any other named cell. Then at weaving time, we know how to
style it (with a numeric superscript).

#+name: f:footnoteLinkParser
#+begin_src haskell
footnoteLinkParser :: OrgParser Link
footnoteLinkParser = do
  name <- footnoteReferenceParser
  orgParserState <- get
  displayName <- case lookup name orgParserState.footnoteNumbers of
    Nothing -> do
      let newFootnoteNumber = orgParserState.footnoteNumber + 1
          newFootnoteNumbers =
            insert name newFootnoteNumber orgParserState.footnoteNumbers
      modify
        ( \s ->
            s
              { footnoteNumber = newFootnoteNumber,
                footnoteNumbers = newFootnoteNumbers
              }
        )
      pure $ T.show newFootnoteNumber
    Just n -> do
      pure $ T.show n
  pure
    $ Link
      { target = LinkTargetFootnote name,
        name = [StyledText {body = displayName, style = styleMaskFrom []}]
      }
#+end_src

As for the footnote definitions, they are expected to be placed on their own, as
separate paragraphs. The definition may only be made up of 1 paragraph, because
good footnotes are short and to the point. For purposes of parsing, it doesn't
actually matter where these definitions are placed, because the order of when
they are defined does not affect how they are presented when woven.

#+name: data:Footnote
#+begin_src haskell
type Footnote :: Type
data Footnote = Footnote
  { name :: Text,
    body :: Prose
  }
  deriving stock (Eq, Ord, Generic, Show)
  deriving anyclass (FromJSON, ToJSON)
#+end_src

Parsing footnote definitions is a little different than other cells, because the
order in which the footnotes are defined may not necessarily be the order in
which the footnotes are referenced from the rest of the document (this can
happen, for example, if you move around the footnote definitions).

For simplicity, Lilac doesn't care about the order in which footnote definitions
appear. However, it does expect two conditions:

  1) Footnote definitions must appear together, just once, in the entire
     document. That is, you may not define footnotes in multiple places.

  2) Inline footnotes (where footnotes are defined in the same area where they
     are referenced) are not allowed.

During weaving, the footnote definitions are sorted according to the order in
which they are referenced (via footnote links).

The footnotes are not turned into cells; instead they are used to build up the
~footnoteDefinitions~ map in [[data:OrgParserState]], which are then dumped into
[[data:OrgDoc]]. This is because there is no point in preserving information about
the order in which these footnote definitions are defined; in other words, it's
useless to store footnote definitions as individual cells in the order they're
defined, if during weave time we are going to sort them anyway in a (possibly)
different order.

#+name: f:footnoteDefinitionParser
#+begin_src haskell
footnoteDefinitionParser :: OrgParser Cell
footnoteDefinitionParser = do
  name <- footnoteReferenceParser
  _ <- spaces
  modify (\s -> s {insideFootnoteDefinition = Just name})
  (CParagraph prose) <- paragraphParser
  orgParserState <- get
  o <- MP.getOffset
  when
    ( (not orgParserState.prevCellIsFootnote)
        && (orgParserState.footnoteDefinitions /= M.empty)
    )
    $ do
      MP.setOffset o
      fail "cannot have more than one group of footnote definitions"
  modify
    ( \s ->
        s
          { footnoteDefinitions =
              insert name prose orgParserState.footnoteDefinitions,
            insideFootnoteDefinition = Nothing
          }
    )
  pure $ CFootnote Footnote {name = name, body = prose}
  where
    spaces :: OrgParser Text
    spaces = MP.takeWhile1P (Just "space") (\c -> c == ' ')
#+end_src

The document is expected to have only one contiguous group of footnote
definitions; if footnote definitions have anything substantive in-between,
that's a parse error.

When we have finished parsing the document, it's expected that all footnote
links and definitions line up with each other (that there is an exact 1:1
match). If there are either links that don't have an associated reference or a
definition without an associated link, it's considered an error.

#+name: f:reconcileFootnotes
#+begin_src haskell
reconcileFootnotes :: OrgParser (Map Text Int)
reconcileFootnotes = do
  orgParserState <- get
  let referenced = M.keysSet orgParserState.footnoteNumbers
      defined = M.keysSet orgParserState.footnoteDefinitions
      brokenLinks = Set.difference referenced defined
      unusedDefs = Set.difference defined referenced
      hasBrokenLinks = length brokenLinks /= 0
      hasUnusedDefs = length unusedDefs /= 0
      brokenLinksMsg = T.unpack . unwords $ toList brokenLinks
      unusedDefsMsg = T.unpack . unwords $ toList unusedDefs
  if
    | hasBrokenLinks -> fail $ "broken footnote links: " <> brokenLinksMsg
    | hasUnusedDefs -> fail $ "unused footnote definitions: " <> unusedDefsMsg
    | otherwise -> pure orgParserState.footnoteNumbers
#+end_src

*** Tests

Check for broken links.

#+name: ti:0013-footnotes-broken-link
#+header: :tangle test/Lilac/ParseSpec/0013-footnotes-broken-link
#+begin_src org
Hello [fn:hello] world.
#+end_src

#+name: t:0013-footnotes-broken-link
#+begin_src haskell
shouldFailWithMessage
  "0013-footnotes-broken-link"
  "broken footnote links: hello"
  24
#+end_src

Check for unused definitions.

#+name: ti:0013-footnotes-unused-def
#+header: :tangle test/Lilac/ParseSpec/0013-footnotes-unused-def
#+begin_src org
Hello world.

[fn:hello] Foo.
#+end_src

#+name: t:0013-footnotes-unused-def
#+begin_src haskell
shouldFailWithMessage
  "0013-footnotes-unused-def"
  "unused footnote definitions: hello"
  30
#+end_src

Basic happy path.

#+name: ti:0013-footnotes-simple
#+header: :tangle test/Lilac/ParseSpec/0013-footnotes-simple
#+begin_src org
Hello [fn:hello] world.

[fn:hello] Foo.
#+end_src

#+name: Expected cells for 0013-footnotes-simple
#+begin_src haskell
[ CParagraph
    $ Prose
      [ ptext "Hello" [],
        PTokenLink
          Link
