          MathFragment
            { parentOid = nilOid,
              meta =
                fromList
                  [ ("__equation_number", "3"),
                    ("name", "eq:y")
                  ],
              body = "1 + 1 = 2"
            }
      ]
      []
  )
  defaultOrgParserState
    { equationNumber = 3,
      anonEquationNumber = 1,
      cellNames =
        Set.fromList
          [ "eq:x",
            "eq:y"
          ]
    }
#+end_src

Standalone math fragments may be included as part of a list item.

#+name: ti:0010-standalone-math-inside-lists
#+header: :tangle test/Lilac/ParseSpec/0010-standalone-math-inside-lists
#+begin_src org
  1) foo

     ,#+name: eq:x
     \[
     2 + 2 = 4
     \]

     ,#+name: eq:y
     \begin{equation}
     1 + 1 = 2
     \end{equation}
#+end_src

#+name: t:0010-standalone-math-inside-lists
#+begin_src haskell
shouldParseWithState
  "0010-standalone-math-inside-lists"
  ( nilOrgDoc
      [ CListItem
          [ LOrderedStart,
            LItemStart
          ],
        CParagraph $ Prose [ptext "foo" []],
        CMathFragment
          MathFragment
            { parentOid = nilOid,
              meta =
                fromList
                  [ ("name", "eq:x")
                  ],
              body = "2 + 2 = 4"
            },
        CMathFragment
          MathFragment
            { parentOid = nilOid,
              meta =
                fromList
                  [ ("__equation_number", "1"),
                    ("name", "eq:y")
                  ],
              body = "1 + 1 = 2"
            },
        CListItem
          [ LItemEnd,
            LOrderedEnd
          ]
      ]
      []
  )
  defaultOrgParserState
    { equationNumber = 1,
      cellNames =
        Set.fromList
          [ "eq:x",
            "eq:y"
          ]
    }
#+end_src

* Figures
:PROPERTIES:
:ID:       a090bf9c-0e52-422b-b0f0-51e3b1bf1698
:END:

A [[data:Figure][Figure]] is just a link to an image file, preceded by some metadata.

#+name: data:Figure
#+begin_src haskell
type Figure :: Type
data Figure = Figure
  { parentOid :: OID,
    meta :: Map Text Text,
    caption :: Prose,
    link :: Link
  }
  deriving stock (Eq, Ord, Generic, Show)
  deriving anyclass (FromJSON, ToJSON)
#+end_src

The ~parentOid~ is just like the one in [[data:Block]].

The metadata (~meta~) recognizes certain reserved keys:

  - type :: (Required) Used to force there to be at least 1 key/value pair in
    the metadata. Otherwise when the parser comes across a link to an image on
    its own without metadata, it is ambiguous whether the link is part of a
    1-link paragraph cell, or as part of a figure cell.

  - name :: (Optional) Used to name the cell uniquely in the surrounding Org
    file. Other cells can refer to this figure by this name (or as part of a
    global [[id:f8254d58-7a3d-4595-a265-e0d6255e6ab5::data:LinkOid][data:LinkOid]]).

  - caption :: (Optional) Displayed as the caption for the image. If there is no
    caption, on weaving the only name of the figure will be just the figure
    number, like "Figure 1" or "Figure 12". You can use styled text (*bold*, etc),
    links, and inline math, just like regular paragraphs (the only caveat is
    that you are not allowed to have newlines).

  - width :: (Optional) Width of the image (as a percentage of page width or
    pixel count), for HTML weaving.

  - height :: (Optional) Height of the image (as a percentage of page width or
    pixel count), for HTML weaving.

Below is an example of a figure:

#+name: fig:PNG image
#+caption: [[https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/PNG][Portable Network Graphics]] sample image.
#+type: figure
#+width: 50%
#+height: 40
[[file:image/PNG-Gradient.png]]

Below is the same, but the figure does not have a ~name~ in the metadata, nor a
~caption~. This is still valid.

#+type: figure
#+width: 50%
#+height: 40
[[file:image/PNG-Gradient.png]]

The only practical difference is that the unnamed figure is not able to be
referenced using a [[id:f8254d58-7a3d-4595-a265-e0d6255e6ab5::data:LinkOid][data:LinkOid]] by this or other Org files.

** Figure parser

First we have to parse for the metadata, and then the link. We don't allow
figures inside list items, because that's just weird (how are you going to have
metadata for that list item?).

For the link target itself, just check that it's pointing to a file (instead of
accepting any link).

#+name: f:figureParser
#+begin_src haskell
figureParser :: OrgParser Cell
figureParser = do
  orgParserState <- get
  when (orgParserState.listLevels /= [])
    $ fail "figures are not allowed inside list items"
  _ <- MP.try $ MP.lookAhead (metaParser >> fileLinkParser >> endOfCell)
  metaKVs <- metaParser
  when (lookup "type" metaKVs /= Just "figure")
    $ fail "type must be 'figure'"
  caption <- case lookup "caption" metaKVs of
    Nothing -> pure $ Prose []
    Just s -> case MP.runParser (runStateT captionParser orgParserState) "" s of
      Left err -> fail $ MP.errorBundlePretty err
      Right (prose, _orgParserState) -> pure prose
  link <- fileLinkParser
  let newFigureNumber = orgParserState.figureNumber + 1
      figureNumberMeta = ("__figure_number", T.show newFigureNumber)
  case link.target of
    LinkTargetOid {}
    LinkTargetLineLabel {}
    LinkTargetURI {}
    LinkTargetFootnote {} -> fail "'file:...' required for link to figures"
    LinkTargetFilePath _ -> do
      modify (\s -> s {figureNumber = newFigureNumber})
      pure
        . CFigure
        $ Figure
          { parentOid =
              firstParentOid orgParserState.docOid orgParserState.headingOidStack,
            meta =
              foldl'
                (\acc (k, v) -> insert k v acc)
                (delete "type" metaKVs)
                [figureNumberMeta],
            caption = caption,
            link = link
          }
  where
    endOfCell :: OrgParser ()
    endOfCell =
      MP.choice
        [ void . MP.try $ string "\n\n",
          char '\n' >> MP.eof
        ]
#+end_src

It's important that we expect the end of the cell (double newline or newline and
~MP.eof~), because otherwise we'll end up parsing the link at the start of a
paragraph cell as a figure. This parser can be even more strict with the input
(as it should be the case anyway for figures), but for now we keep things
simple with this ~endOfCell~ rule.

The metadata is parsed with =metaParser= which relies on =metaEntryParser=. We make
sure that the name given to the cell is unique inside the document being parsed,
because ultimately these cell names determine how we generate HTML IDs for each
cell (although we could give machine-generated ID names, we prefer to use the
name if one exists because they are usually much more human-friendly).

#+name: f:metaParser
#+begin_src haskell
metaParser :: OrgParser (Map Text Text)
metaParser = do
  metaKVs <- many $ lexeme metaEntryParser
  let metaKVsMap = fromList metaKVs
  case lookup "name" metaKVsMap of
    Just cellName -> assertUniqueCellName cellName
    Nothing -> pure ()
  pure metaKVsMap
#+end_src

The [[f:metaEntryParser]] is similar to [[f:docMetaEntryParser]]. In the latter, we use
=avoidParsingBlockMetadata= to avoid touching on the toes of =blockMetaEntryParser=,
but here we don't need to do that because we use =lookAhead= in [[f:figureParser]] to
look for a link that follows metadata --- so we are guaranteed (after we parse
some metadata) that it will only be for a link (figure), not a [[data:Block]] or
some other type.

We still have to check that each metadata line does not start with =#+begin_=
because otherwise this will also succeed for blocks (even inside the =lookAhead=
in [[f:figureParser]]).

#+name: f:metaEntryParser
#+begin_src haskell
metaEntryParser :: OrgParser (Text, Text)
metaEntryParser = do
  mapM_
    MP.notFollowedBy
    $ map
      string
      [ "#+begin_",
        "#+print_bibliography:",
        "#+print_glossary:"
      ]
  _ <- string "#+"
  key <- MP.takeWhile1P (Just "(meta) keyword letter") isKeywordLetter
  _ <- MP.label "(meta) separator" $ string ": "
  _ <- many $ char ' '
  value <-
    if key == "name"
      then do
        first <- MP.satisfy (\c -> c /= '(' && isValueLetter c) -- (ref:non-line-label name)
        rest <- MP.takeWhileP (Just "(meta) value letter") isValueLetter
        pure $ T.cons first rest
      else MP.takeWhile1P (Just "(meta) value letter") isValueLetter
  pure (key, value)
  where
    isKeywordLetter c = or $ map ($ c) [isAsciiLower, (== '_')]
    isValueLetter c = c /= '\n'
#+end_src

For the ~#+name~ key, we prohibit the use of the opening parenthesis as the first
character of the name at [[(non-line-label name)]], because then that would make
~[[(foo)]]~ ambiguous (it could refer to either something named ~(foo)~, or it could
refer to a [[id:da4aa2a7-3896-4969-aa5e-5e3d842c35db][line label]] ~(ref:foo)~.

#+name: ti:0007-blocks-no-name-with-opening-parenthesis
#+header: :tangle test/Lilac/ParseSpec/0007-blocks-no-name-with-opening-parenthesis
#+header: :line-label off
#+begin_src org
,#+name: (a)
,#+begin_src sh
a
,#+end_src
#+end_src

#+name: t:0007-blocks-no-name-with-opening-parenthesis
#+begin_src haskell
shouldFailWithError
  "0007-blocks-no-name-with-opening-parenthesis"
  (utoks "(" <> etoks " ")
  8
#+end_src

** Tests

Below we parse a doc that has figures and blocks, both with metadata.

#+name: ti:0011-figures-and-blocks
#+header: :tangle test/Lilac/ParseSpec/0011-figures-and-blocks
#+begin_src org
,#+name: f:f
,#+header: :xxx bar
,#+begin_src haskell
f = 1 + 1
,#+end_src

,#+name: fig:hello
,#+caption: =hello=
,#+type: figure
,#+width: 100%
[[file:image/hello.png]]

,#+name: f:g
,#+header: :yyy bar
,#+begin_src haskell
g x = x + 1
,#+end_src

,#+type: figure
[[file:image/world.png]]

  - [[file:not-figure]]

[[file:not-a-figure-2]]

[[id:00000000-0000-0000-0000-000000000000::not a figure]]

,#+type: figure
[[file:foo.png]]
#+end_src

#+name: t:0011-figures-and-blocks
#+begin_src haskell
shouldParseWithState
  "0011-figures-and-blocks"
  ( nilOrgDoc
      [ CBlock
          Block
            { parentOid = nilOid,
              meta =
                fromList
                  [ ("__block_type", "src"),
                    ("__src_language", "haskell"),
                    ("name", "f:f"),
                    ("xxx", "bar")
                  ],
              body = [BTokenString "f = 1 + 1"]
            },
        CFigure
          Figure
            { parentOid = nilOid,
              meta =
                fromList
                  [ ("__figure_number", "1"),
                    ("width", "100%"),
                    ("name", "fig:hello"),
                    ("caption", "=hello=")
                  ],
              caption =
                Prose
                  [ ptext "hello" [Verbatim]
                  ],
              link =
                Link
                  { target = LinkTargetFilePath "image/hello.png",
                    name = [stext "image/hello.png" []]
                  }
            },
        CBlock
          Block
            { parentOid = nilOid,
              meta =
                fromList
                  [ ("__block_type", "src"),
                    ("__src_language", "haskell"),
                    ("name", "f:g"),
                    ("yyy", "bar")
                  ],
              body = [BTokenString "g x = x + 1"]
            },
        CFigure
          Figure
            { parentOid = nilOid,
              meta =
                fromList
                  [ ("__figure_number", "2")
                  ],
              caption = Prose [],
              link =
                Link
                  { target = LinkTargetFilePath "image/world.png",
                    name = [stext "image/world.png" []]
                  }
            },
        CListItem
          [ LUnorderedStart,
            LItemStart

        CParagraph
          $ Prose
            [ PTokenLink
                Link
                  { target = LinkTargetFilePath "not-figure",
                    name = [stext "not-figure" []]

            ],

          [ LItemEnd,
            LUnorderedEnd
          ],
        CParagraph
          $ Prose
            [ PTokenLink
                Link
                  { target = LinkTargetFilePath "not-a-figure-2",
                    name = [stext "not-a-figure-2" []]
                  }
            ],
        CParagraph
          $ Prose
            [ PTokenLink
                Link
                  { target =
                      LinkTargetOid
                        LinkOid
                          { oid = nilOid,
                            name = "not a figure"
                          },
                    name = [stext "not a figure" []]
                  }
            ],
        CFigure
          Figure
            { parentOid = nilOid,
              meta =
                fromList
                  [ ("__figure_number", "3")

              caption = Prose [],

                  { target = LinkTargetFilePath "foo.png",
                    name = [stext "foo.png" []]

* Tables
:PROPERTIES:
:ID:       d0f60631-8b30-4ae1-992d-49c7b0ea38fb
:END:

Tables are like [[data:Figure][figures]] in that they have some metadata. Unlike a figure which
is just a link, a table has a two-dimensional grid with some information in each
row/column. The requirement is that the first non-whitespace character starts
with a vertical bar (=|=), also known as the pipe character in UNIX.

All tables are numbered. A table can also have a caption (just like a
[[data:Figure]]) using metadata.

#+name: data:Table
#+begin_src haskell
type Table :: Type
data Table = Table
  { parentOid :: OID,
    meta :: Map Text Text,
    caption :: Prose,
    grid :: Map (Int, Int) Prose
  }
  deriving stock (Eq, Ord, Generic, Show)
  deriving anyclass (FromJSON, ToJSON)
#+end_src

The ~parentOid~ is just like the one in [[data:Block]].

Below is an example of a table:

#+name: tbl:Programming languages
#+caption: Programming languages and typing disciplines.
| Language | Typing          |
|----------+-----------------|
| LISP     | dynamic, strong |
| C        | static, weak    |
| Haskell  | static, strong  |

Because we named the above with a ~#+name~ keyword, we can refer to it as
[[tbl:Programming languages]].

The very first line of the table determines the number of expected columns. Org
syntax allows for horizontal lines inside the table like ~|---+-----|~, but these
are ignored.

#+name: f:tableParser
#+begin_src haskell
tableParser :: OrgParser Cell
tableParser = do
  orgParserState <- get
  _ <- MP.try $ MP.lookAhead (metaParser >> tableRowParser)
  metaKVs <- metaParser
  caption <- case lookup "caption" metaKVs of
    Nothing -> pure $ Prose []
    Just s -> case MP.runParser (runStateT captionParser orgParserState) "" s of
      Left err -> fail $ MP.errorBundlePretty err
      Right (prose, _orgParserState) -> pure prose
  rows <- MP.some $ tableLineParser <|> tableRowParser
  _ <- MP.optional tableFormulaParser
  let newTableNumber = orgParserState.tableNumber + 1
  modify (\s -> s {tableNumber = newTableNumber})
  pure
    . CTable
    $ Table
      { parentOid =
          firstParentOid orgParserState.docOid orgParserState.headingOidStack,
        meta = insert "__table_number" (T.show newTableNumber) metaKVs,
        caption = caption,
        grid = fromList $ rowsToTuples rows
      }
#+end_src

The caption parser parses the caption text from a ~#+caption: ...~ into prose.
This way the caption can support other text styles, links, and even math
fragments. We parse twice (by invoking ~MP.runParser~), because it's easy to
implement; if we wanted a smoother experience for the user, we would have to
enrich the [[f:metaParser]] to hold values that can be either raw text or prose.

#+name: f:captionParser
#+begin_src haskell
captionParser :: OrgParser Prose
captionParser = do
  caption <- proseParser $ whitespaceCompressor spaceConsumer
  failIfLeftoverActiveStyles
  pure caption
  where
    spaceConsumer :: OrgParser Text
    spaceConsumer = MP.takeWhile1P (Just "space") (== ' ')
#+end_src

The [[f:tableFormulaParser]] checks for a table formula line, which is neither
useful for tangling or weaving. These table formulas always come *after* a table,
not before it. We need to be able to parse it in order to ignore it (otherwise
we will think that the ~#+tblfm: ...~ is part of another Org cell's metadata).

#+name: f:tableFormulaParser
#+begin_src haskell
tableFormulaParser :: OrgParser ()
tableFormulaParser = do
  _ <- string "#+"
  _ <- string "tblfm: " <|> string "TBLFM: "
  _ <- MP.takeWhile1P (Just "table formula") (/= '\n')
  _ <- char '\n'
  pure ()
#+end_src

The [[f:tableLineParser]] skips over horizontal rules that typically separate the
table heading (column names) from the rest of the rows that hold the actual
data. It also skips over any leading indentation in the subsequent line, based
on the ~continuationIndent~ which is set by [[f:listHeadIndentLevelParser]]. This way,
we can parse tables inside list items.

#+name: f:tableLineParser
#+begin_src haskell
tableLineParser :: OrgParser [Prose]
tableLineParser = do
  _ <- string "|-"
  _ <- MP.takeWhile1P (Just "table line characters") isTableLineChar
  _ <- char '\n'
  orgParserState <- get
  _ <-
    MP.label "indentation for next table line"
      $ MP.count orgParserState.continuationIndent (char ' ')
  pure [tableHorizontalLine]
  where
    isTableLineChar c = c == '-' || c == '+' || c == '|'
#+end_src

The [[f:tableHorizontalLine]] is a special value to signify a horizontal line, which
can basically only be generated internally because of the trailing NULL byte.

#+name: f:tableHorizontalLine
#+begin_src haskell
tableHorizontalLine :: Prose
tableHorizontalLine =
  Prose
    [ PTokenStyledText
        StyledText {body = "horizontal-line\NUL", style = styleMaskFrom []}
    ]
#+end_src

The [[f:tableRowParser]] parses rows with data in them. It also skips over
indentation found in the subsequent line (if any). Unlike ~tableLineParser~, we
wrap this indentation parsing with ~MP.optional~ because we cannot tell if there
are more table rows after this or not. Contrast this with the ~tableLineParser~,
which is never expected to be the last line of a table.

#+name: f:tableRowParser
#+begin_src haskell
tableRowParser :: OrgParser [Prose]
tableRowParser = do
  _ <- char '|'
  _ <- spaces
  tableCells <- MP.some $ emptyTableCellParser <|> tableCellParser
  _ <- char '\n'
  orgParserState <- get
  _ <-
    MP.optional
      . MP.label "indentation for next table line"
      $ MP.count orgParserState.continuationIndent (char ' ')
  pure tableCells
  where
    emptyTableCellParser :: OrgParser Prose
    emptyTableCellParser = do
      _ <- char '|'
      _ <- MP.optional spaces
      pure $ Prose []
    spaces :: OrgParser Text
    spaces = MP.takeWhile1P (Just "table whitespace") (== ' ')
#+end_src

The ~emptyTableCellParser~ parses an empty table cell (because [[f:proseParser]]
requires non-empty text). We need this in order to parse (essentially skip over)
empty table cells, while still recording their existence. See how empty table
cells are parsed in [[t:0012-tables-and-prose]].

The [[f:tableCellParser]] just parses text inside a single row and column
combination --- which we call a "table cell". The main idea is that we parse
each table cell, which may not contain pipe characters in it (otherwise it
confuses =org-mode= in Emacs, which results in the table becoming formatted with
extra columns that we don't want).

#+name: f:tableCellParser
#+begin_src haskell
tableCellParser :: OrgParser Prose
tableCellParser = do
  prose <- onlyMarkingCharacterParser <|> (proseParser $ whitespaceCompressor spacesInsideProse)
  _ <- MP.optional spaces
  _ <- char '|'
  _ <- MP.optional spaces
  pure $ compressProse prose
  where
    spaces :: OrgParser Text
    spaces = MP.takeWhile1P (Just "table whitespace") (== ' ')
    spacesInsideProse :: OrgParser Text
    spacesInsideProse = do
      _ <- MP.try $ MP.lookAhead (spaces >> tableCellContent)
      spaces
    tableCellContent :: OrgParser ()
    tableCellContent = void $ MP.noneOf (" \n|" :: String)
    onlyMarkingCharacterParser :: OrgParser Prose
    onlyMarkingCharacterParser = do
      c <- MP.satisfy (`elem` markingChars)
      _ <- MP.try . MP.lookAhead $ do
        _ <- MP.takeWhile1P (Just "table whitespace (marking char)") (== ' ')
        _ <- char '|'
        pure ()
      pure
        $ Prose
          [ PTokenStyledText
              StyledText {body = T.singleton c, style = styleMaskFrom []}
          ]
#+end_src

The ~spacesInsideProse~ is a special space consumer inside table cells, where
newlines and pipe characters are prohibited. This way, when [[f:proseParser]] uses
it to find some contiguous amount of text, it will know to stop when it sees the
pipe character (as that function does not treat pipe characters differently than
any other non-space character).

The ~onlyMarkingCharacterParser~ is there to parse [[id:3b072b1b-c63e-4fca-ab56-9388d11aa3c0::f:markingChars][f:markingChars]] which share the
same syntax as the characters used for styling prose, especially ~_~, ~*~, and ~/~.
These characters will break things if we let [[f:proseParser]] naively look at it,
because that parser has no concept of table cell boundaries. The
~onlyMarkingCharacterParser~ parses one of these marking characters, and creates
the corresponding [[newtype:Prose]] value.

After the rows are parsed, we need to convert them to a =grid= (as part of
[[data:Table]]. We do this with [[f:rowsToTuples]].

#+name: f:rowsToTuples
#+begin_src haskell
rowsToTuples :: [[Prose]] -> [((Int, Int), Prose)]
rowsToTuples rows = concatMap rowToTuples $ zip [1 ..] rows
#+end_src

We assign each row its row index with ~zip [1 ..]~, then delegate most of the work
to [[f:rowToTuples]] where each row's table cells are given its column index to form
a completed table cell. These are simply accumulated and returned as a list. The
order of accumulation (consing) does not matter because this is ultimately fed
into a =Map= type, an unordered container.

#+name: f:rowToTuples
#+begin_src haskell
rowToTuples :: (Int, [Prose]) -> [((Int, Int), Prose)]
rowToTuples (rowIndex, row)
  | null row = [((rowIndex, 1), Prose [])]
  | otherwise = fst $ foldl' f ([], 1) row
  where
    f :: ([((Int, Int), Prose)], Int) -> Prose -> ([((Int, Int), Prose)], Int)
    f (acc, colIndex) tableCell =
      ( ((rowIndex, colIndex), tableCell) : acc,
        colIndex + 1
      )
#+end_src

** Check for numeric table cell

It's useful to know if a table cell contains mostly numeric content, at the time
of weaving (see [[id:6c10afe2-2714-4e85-9121-e96a36d27ba4::f:weaveTable][f:weaveTable]]). This way, we can right-align the contents of that
table cell, just like popular spreadsheet programs.

[[f:isNumericText]] parses the input through [[f:isNumericTextParser]].

#+name: f:isNumericText
#+begin_src haskell
isNumericText :: Text -> Bool
isNumericText input = case MP.runParser
  (runStateT isNumericTextParser defaultOrgParserState)
  ""
  input of
  Left _ -> False
  Right (b, _) -> b
#+end_src

[[f:isNumericTextParser]] allows for a currency symbol, which can be either after or
before a ~-~ or ~+~ sign. Then any combination of digits, periods, commas, and
spaces are allowed. We check that there is at least 1 numeric digit in the
string.

#+name: f:isNumericTextParser
#+begin_src haskell
isNumericTextParser :: OrgParser Bool
isNumericTextParser = do
  _ <- MP.optional sign
  _ <- MP.optional $ MP.satisfy isCurrencySymbol
  _ <- MP.optional sign
  s <- MP.some $ MP.satisfy isDigit <|> MP.satisfy (`elem` ['.', ',', ' '])
  MP.eof
  let numberFound = (> 0) . length $ filter isDigit s
  pure numberFound
  where
    isCurrencySymbol c = generalCategory c == CurrencySymbol
    sign :: OrgParser Char
    sign = MP.satisfy (`elem` ['-', '+'])
#+end_src

** Tests

The test below checks if we can parse a table with all 3 types of prose text
(styled text, links, and math fragments).

#+name: ti:0012-tables-and-prose
#+header: :tangle test/Lilac/ParseSpec/0012-tables-and-prose
#+begin_src org
,#+name: tbl:famous-equations
,#+caption: *Bold*. [[tbl:famous-equations][A self-link]]. \(E = mc^2\)
| Name            | Equation            | Link |
|-----------------+---------------------+------|
| Pythagoras \vert{} Foo | \(a^2 + b^2 = c^2\) | [[https://wiki/x][Wiki]] |
| Albert *Einstein* | \(E = mc^2\) | [[https://wiki/y][Wiki]] |
| Unknown           |  |  |
| _ | * | / |
,#+tblfm: $x
#+end_src

#+name: t:0012-tables-and-prose
#+begin_src haskell
shouldParseWithState
  "0012-tables-and-prose"
  ( nilOrgDoc
      <<Expected cells for 0012-tables-and-prose>>
      []
  )
  defaultOrgParserState
    { cellNames =
        Set.fromList
          [ "tbl:famous-equations"
          ],
      tableNumber = 1
    }
#+end_src

#+name: Expected cells for 0012-tables-and-prose
#+begin_src haskell
[ CTable
    Table
      { parentOid = nilOid,
        meta =
          fromList
            [ ("name", "tbl:famous-equations"),
              ("caption", "*Bold*. [[tbl:famous-equations][A self-link]]. \\(E = mc^2\\)"),
              ("__table_number", "1")
            ],
        caption =
          Prose
            [ ptext "Bold" [Bold],
              ptext "." [],
              ptext " " [],
              PTokenLink
                Link
                  { name =
                      [ stext "A" [],
                        stext " " [],
                        stext "self-link" []
                      ],
                    target =
                      LinkTargetOid
                        LinkOid {oid = nilOid, name = "tbl:famous-equations"}
                  },
              ptext "." [],
              ptext " " [],
              PTokenMathFragment
                MathFragment {parentOid = nilOid, meta = fromList [], body = "E = mc^2"}
            ],
        grid =
          fromList
            [ ( (1, 1),
                Prose [ptext "Name" []]
              ),
              ( (1, 2),
                Prose [ptext "Equation" []]
              ),
              ( (1, 3),
                Prose [ptext "Link" []]
              ),
              ( (2, 1),
                L.tableHorizontalLine
              ),
              ( (3, 1),
                Prose [ptext "Pythagoras | Foo" []]
              ),
              ( (3, 2),
                Prose
                  [ PTokenMathFragment
                      MathFragment
                        { parentOid = nilOid,
                          meta = fromList [],
                          body = "a^2 + b^2 = c^2"
                        }
                  ]
              ),
              ( (3, 3),
                Prose
                  [ PTokenLink
                      Link
                        { target =
                            LinkTargetURI
                              N.URI
                                { N.uriScheme = "https:",
                                  N.uriAuthority =
                                    Just
                                      N.URIAuth
                                        { N.uriUserInfo = "",
                                          N.uriRegName = "wiki",
                                          N.uriPort = ""
                                        },
                                  N.uriPath = "/x",
                                  N.uriQuery = "",
                                  N.uriFragment = ""
                                },
                          name = [stext "Wiki" []]
                        }
                  ]
              ),
              ( (4, 1),
                Prose
                  [ ptext "Albert " [],
                    ptext "Einstein" [Bold]
                  ]
              ),
              ( (4, 2),
                Prose
                  [ PTokenMathFragment
                      MathFragment
                        { parentOid = nilOid,
                          meta = fromList [],
                          body = "E = mc^2"
                        }
                  ]
              ),
              ( (4, 3),
                Prose
                  [ PTokenLink
                      Link
                        { target =
                            LinkTargetURI
                              N.URI
                                { N.uriScheme = "https:",
                                  N.uriAuthority =
                                    Just
                                      N.URIAuth
                                        { N.uriUserInfo = "",
                                          N.uriRegName = "wiki",
                                          N.uriPort = ""
                                        },
                                  N.uriPath = "/y",
                                  N.uriQuery = "",
                                  N.uriFragment = ""
                                },
                          name = [stext "Wiki" []]
                        }
                  ]
              ),
              ((5, 1), Prose [ptext "Unknown" []]),
              ((5, 2), Prose []),
              ((5, 3), Prose []),
              ((6, 1), Prose [ptext "_" []]),
              ((6, 2), Prose [ptext "*" []]),
              ((6, 3), Prose [ptext "/" []])
            ]
      }
]
#+end_src

* Testing framework
:PROPERTIES:
:ID:       ebf0d269-79d3-408a-a6e7-baf720d73837
:END:

The tests here are all designed to read from Org source documents. There are
some benefits of writing tests this way:

  1) The raw input file (on disk) for test cases can be looked at if necessary.
     This is especially useful if the input has any special Org characters in it
     we have to escape. See [[ti:0007-blocks-ending]] and
     [[ti:0004-properties-drawer-empty]] where we need to escape some parts of the input with a
     leading comma (so as to distinguish between the Org syntax of this document
     vs the nested Org syntax of the input file we are defining).

  2) We can organize the input files in a systematic way. We use a
     ~NNNN-<description>~ naming scheme for the inputs, which all live under
     [[file:../test/Lilac/ParseSpec]]. Each unique ~NNNN~ number groups together files
     meant to test similar functionality. This makes it easy to see at a glance
     what sorts of things we test.

The downside is that we have to perform lots of small read operations (IO) to
run the unit tests. But this cost is small enough that we can ignore it (we
spend far more time compiling the tests themselves).

** Test module and helpers
:PROPERTIES:
:ID:       906bcb25-4380-4e41-a941-e57cbcf7d2a1
:END:

#+header: :tangle test/Lilac/ParseSpec.hs

We have to export a function named ~spec~ for discovery of test cases to happen
automatically, so we define it in [[parseSpec discovery]].

#+name: parseSpec discovery
#+begin_src haskell
spec :: Spec
spec = parseSpec
#+end_src

Most of the test helpers are concerned about reading a raw input file from disk,
and then running the [[f:orgDocParser]] against it.

#+name: ParseSpec helpers
#+begin_src haskell
type Parser :: Type -> Type
type Parser = <<Concrete type for Megaparsec>>

<<f:shouldParseWith>>
<<f:shouldParseWithState>>
<<f:shouldFailWithMessage>>
<<f:shouldFailWithError>>
<<f:shouldFail>>
<<f:plainParagraph>>
<<f:plainHeading>>
<<f:nilOrgDoc>>
<<f:checkTextToProseRoundTrip>>
#+end_src

The ~Parser~ type synonym uses the same [[Concrete type for Megaparsec][concrete types]] as we do for
[[type:OrgParser]]. The ~Parser~ type synonym is used in tests such as
[[t:0005-notFollowedBy-behavior-for-empty-input]].

We need a helper function around =parseOrgDoc=, which will read in a file from a
given path (to feed to =parseOrgDoc=). We name this helper [[f:shouldParseWith]].

#+name: f:shouldParseWith
#+begin_src haskell
shouldParseWith :: FilePath -> OrgDoc -> Spec
shouldParseWith path expected = do
  before (LU.readFrom "test/Lilac/ParseSpec/" path) $ do
    it path check
  where
    check input
      | expected.oid == nilOid =
          action nilOidOrgDoc input defaultOrgParserState
      | otherwise = action "" input defaultOrgParserState
    action inputPrefix input expectedState =
      MP.runParser
        (runStateT L.orgDocParser defaultOrgParserState)
        path
        (inputPrefix <> input)
        `shouldParse` (expected, expectedState)
#+end_src

For convenience though, [[f:shouldParseWith]] prefixes the file with a required Org
ID, with the help of [[f:nilOidOrgDoc]]. This way the majority of test cases don't
have to worry about this detail. The ones that do have to check the behavior of
the top-level properties drawer have to do it manually, such as in
[[t:0002-doc-metadata-properties-drawer-clash]].

#+name: f:nilOidOrgDoc
#+begin_src haskell
nilOidOrgDoc :: Text
nilOidOrgDoc =
  """
  :PROPERTIES:
  :ID:       00000000-0000-0000-0000-000000000000
  :END:

  """
#+end_src

#+name: f:nilOrgDoc
#+begin_src haskell
nilOrgDoc :: [Cell] -> [PseudoEdge] -> OrgDoc
nilOrgDoc cells pseudoEdges =
  OrgDoc
    { oid = nilOid,
      cells = cells,
      pseudoEdges = pseudoEdges,
      footnoteNumbers = M.empty,
      glossary = M.empty,
      lineLabels = M.empty,
      cslPath = Nothing,
      bibPaths = [],
      citations = [],
      meta = fromList [("ID", "00000000-0000-0000-0000-000000000000")]
    }
#+end_src

If we want to check the state (the parsing operation is stateful), we need to
check the expected end state as well. We do this with =ps= below.

#+name: f:shouldParseWithState
#+begin_src haskell
shouldParseWithState :: FilePath -> OrgDoc -> OrgParserState -> Spec
shouldParseWithState path expected expectedState = do
  before (LU.readFrom "test/Lilac/ParseSpec/" path) $ do
    it path check
  where
    check input
      | expected.oid == nilOid =
          action nilOidOrgDoc input expectedState
      | otherwise = action "" input expectedState
    action inputPrefix input expectedState' =
      MP.runParser
        (runStateT L.orgDocParser defaultOrgParserState)
        path
        (inputPrefix <> input)
        `shouldParse` (expected, expectedState')
#+end_src

Sometimes we want to test that a file will fail to parse. We do this with
[[f:shouldFailWithMessage]].

#+name: f:shouldFailWithMessage
#+begin_src haskell
shouldFailWithMessage :: FilePath -> String -> Int -> Spec
shouldFailWithMessage path errMsg pos = do
  before (LU.readFrom "test/Lilac/ParseSpec/" path) $ do
    it path $ \input -> do
      MP.runParser
        (runStateT L.orgDocParser defaultOrgParserState)
        path
        (inputPrefix <> input)
        `shouldFailWith` (errFancy newPos . fancy $ MP.ErrorFail errMsg)
  where
    inputPrefix = nilOidOrgDoc
    newPos = pos + T.length inputPrefix
#+end_src

Note that we use the =MP.ErrorFail= constructor because that's the only way we can
check for failures in parsers where we manually return an error with
=Control.Monad.fail= (as opposed to Megaparsec itself throwing the error because
of a failure of the given parser combinators to account for the shape of the
input text).

The [[f:shouldFailWithError]] helper checks for failures thrown by Megaparsec (the
ones that [[f:shouldFailWithMessage]] do not check for). The =ET= likely stands for
"trivial errors" or (error "trivial"), because it is documented in upstream as
"Auxiliary type for construction of trivial parse errors.".

#+name: f:shouldFailWithError
#+begin_src haskell
shouldFailWithError :: FilePath -> ET Text -> Int -> Spec
shouldFailWithError path errorTrivial pos = do
  before (LU.readFrom "test/Lilac/ParseSpec/" path) $ do
    it path $ \input -> do
      MP.runParser
        (runStateT L.orgDocParser defaultOrgParserState)
        path
        (inputPrefix <> input)
        `shouldFailWith` (err newPos errorTrivial)
  where
    inputPrefix = nilOidOrgDoc
    newPos = pos + T.length inputPrefix
#+end_src

The [[f:shouldFail]] helper simply asserts that the parse should fail, without regard
to the exact failure message.

#+name: f:shouldFail
#+begin_src haskell
shouldFail :: FilePath -> Spec
shouldFail path = do
  before (LU.readFrom "test/Lilac/ParseSpec/" path) $ do
    it path $ \input -> do
      MP.runParser
        (runStateT L.orgDocParser defaultOrgParserState)
        path
        `shouldFailOn` (inputPrefix <> input)
  where
    inputPrefix = nilOidOrgDoc
#+end_src

The function =plainParagraph= helps to cut down on some of the repetition.

#+name: f:plainParagraph
#+begin_src haskell
plainParagraph :: Text -> Cell
plainParagraph body =
  CParagraph
    $ Prose
      [ PTokenStyledText
          StyledText
            { body = body,
              style = styleMaskFrom []
            }
      ]
#+end_src

The same goes for function ~plainHeading~.

#+name: f:plainHeading
#+begin_src haskell
plainHeading :: Int -> [Int] -> Cell
plainHeading level section =
  CHeading
    Heading
      { level = level,
        section = section,
        body =
          Prose
            [ PTokenStyledText
                StyledText
                  { body = T.pack $ show level,
                    style = styleMaskFrom []
                  }
            ],
        meta = fromList []
      }
#+end_src

* Footnotes

[fn:cell] We deviate from the terminology used in the [[https://orgmode.org/worg/org-syntax.html][Org syntax description]],
where the terms /element/ and /object/ are used for what we call a /cell/. This is to
avoid any unintentional overlap of meaning.

[fn:semigroup-monoid-for-list] For example, see [[https://hackage.haskell.org/package/ghc-internal-9.1201.0/docs/src/GHC.Internal.Base.html#line-713][the implementation for GHC
9.12]] (found via [[https://hackage.haskell.org/package/base-4.21.0.0/docs/Data-Semigroup.html#t:Semigroup][base-4.21.0.0]]).

[fn:megaparsec-errors] We're supposed to use things like ~MP.region~ or
~MP.parseError~ according to the [[https://markkarpov.com/tutorial/megaparsec.html#controlling-location-of-parse-errors][Megaparsec tutorial]], but it doesn't seem to work
(at least with the way we've constructed things with relation to our use of
[[f:listItemsEndParser]] in [[f:cellParser]]).
